<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg/220px-Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="118" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg/330px-Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg/440px-Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2303" data-file-height="1232" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hendrick_Avercamp_-_IJsvermaak_bij_een_stad.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Winter landscape with skaters near the city of <a href="/wiki/Kampen,_Overijssel" title="Kampen, Overijssel">Kampen</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Hendrick_Avercamp" title="Hendrick Avercamp">Hendrick Avercamp</a> (1620s)</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg/220px-Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="130" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg/330px-Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg/440px-Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2505" data-file-height="1475" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Johannes_Lingelbach_001.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Dam_Square" title="Dam Square">Dam Square</a> in 1656</div></div></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Golden_Age" title="Dutch Golden Age">Dutch Golden Age</a>, spanning much of the 17th century, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch Empire</a> grew to become one of the major seafaring and economic powers, alongside Portugal, Spain, France and England. Science, military and art (especially <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Golden_Age_painting" title="Dutch Golden Age painting">painting</a>) were among the most acclaimed in the world. By 1650, the Dutch owned 16,000 merchant ships.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company" title="Dutch East India Company">Dutch East India Company</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_West_India_Company" title="Dutch West India Company">Dutch West India Company</a> established <a href="/wiki/Colonization" title="Colonization">colonies</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trading_post" title="Trading post">trading posts</a> all over the world, including ruling the western parts of <a href="/wiki/Taiwan" title="Taiwan">Taiwan</a> between <a href="/wiki/Taiwan_under_Dutch_rule" class="mw-redirect" title="Taiwan under Dutch rule">1624–1662 and 1664–1667</a>. The <a href="/wiki/New_Netherland" title="New Netherland">Dutch settlement in North America</a> began with the founding of <a href="/wiki/New_Amsterdam" title="New Amsterdam">New Amsterdam</a> on the southern part of <a href="/wiki/Manhattan" title="Manhattan">Manhattan</a> in 1614. In South Africa, the Dutch settled the <a href="/wiki/Cape_Colony" title="Cape Colony">Cape Colony</a> in 1652. <a href="/wiki/Dutch_colonisation_of_the_Guianas" title="Dutch colonisation of the Guianas">Dutch colonies in South America</a> were established along the many rivers in the fertile <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a> plains, among them <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(Dutch_colony)" title="Surinam (Dutch colony)">Colony of Surinam</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">Suriname</a>). In Asia, the Dutch established the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_Indies" title="Dutch East Indies">Dutch East Indies</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>), and the only western trading post in Japan, <a href="/wiki/Dejima" title="Dejima">Dejima</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>During the period of <a href="/wiki/Proto-industrialisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Proto-industrialisation">Proto-industrialisation</a>, the empire received 50% of textiles and 80% of silks import from the India's <a href="/wiki/Mughal_Empire" title="Mughal Empire">Mughal Empire</a>, chiefly from its most developed region known as <a href="/wiki/Bengal_Subah" title="Bengal Subah">Bengal Subah</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tong_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tong-84">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-esposito_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-esposito-85">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Prakash_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prakash-87">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many economic historians regard the Netherlands as the first thoroughly <a href="/wiki/Capitalism" title="Capitalism">capitalist</a> country in the world. In early modern Europe, it had the wealthiest trading city (<a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a>) and the first full-time <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam_Stock_Exchange" class="mw-redirect" title="Amsterdam Stock Exchange">stock exchange</a>. The inventiveness of the traders led to insurance and retirement funds as well as phenomena such as the <a href="/wiki/Boom_and_bust" class="mw-redirect" title="Boom and bust">boom-bust cycle</a>, the world's first <a href="/wiki/Asset_price_inflation" title="Asset price inflation">asset-inflation bubble</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Tulip_mania" title="Tulip mania">tulip mania</a> of 1636–1637, and the world's first <a href="/wiki/Bear_raid" title="Bear raid">bear raider</a>, <a href="/wiki/Isaac_le_Maire" class="mw-redirect" title="Isaac le Maire">Isaac le Maire</a>, who forced prices down by dumping stock and then buying it back at a discount.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> In 1672 – known in Dutch history as the <a href="/wiki/Rampjaar" title="Rampjaar">Rampjaar</a> (Disaster Year) – the Dutch Republic was at war with France, England and three German Bishoprics simultaneously. At sea, it could successfully prevent the English and French navies from entering the western shores. On land, however, it was almost taken over internally by the advancing French and German armies coming from the east. It managed to turn the tide by <a href="/wiki/Hollandic_Water_Line" class="mw-redirect" title="Hollandic Water Line">inundating parts of Holland</a> but could never recover to its former glory again and went into a state of a general decline in the 18th century, with economic competition from England and long-standing rivalries between the two main factions in Dutch society, the republican <a href="/wiki/Dutch_States_Party" title="Dutch States Party"><i>Staatsgezinden</i></a> and the supporters of the <a href="/wiki/Stadtholder" title="Stadtholder">stadtholder</a> the <a href="/wiki/Orangism_(Netherlands)" class="mw-redirect" title="Orangism (Netherlands)"><i>Prinsgezinden</i></a> as main <a href="/wiki/Political_faction" title="Political faction">political factions</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Batavian_Republic_and_Kingdom_.281795.E2.80.931890.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Batavian_Republic_and_Kingdom_(1795–1890)">Batavian Republic and Kingdom (1795–1890)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Batavian_Republic" title="Batavian Republic">Batavian Republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Holland" title="Kingdom of Holland">Kingdom of Holland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_Principality_of_the_United_Netherlands" title="Sovereign Principality of the United Netherlands">Sovereign Principality of the United Netherlands</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="United Kingdom of the Netherlands">United Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a></div>
<p>With the armed support of <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">revolutionary France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Patriots_(Dutch_Republic)" class="mw-redirect" title="Patriots (Dutch Republic)">Dutch republicans</a> proclaimed the <a href="/wiki/Batavian_Republic" title="Batavian Republic">Batavian Republic</a>, modelled after the <a href="/wiki/French_First_Republic" title="French First Republic">French Republic</a> and rendering the Netherlands a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary state</a> on 19 January 1795. The <a href="/wiki/Stadtholder" title="Stadtholder">stadtholder</a> <a href="/wiki/William_V_of_Orange" class="mw-redirect" title="William V of Orange">William V of Orange</a> had fled to England. But from 1806 to 1810, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Holland" title="Kingdom of Holland">Kingdom of Holland</a> was set up by <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon Bonaparte">Napoleon Bonaparte</a> as a puppet kingdom governed by his brother <a href="/wiki/Louis_Bonaparte" title="Louis Bonaparte">Louis Bonaparte</a> to control the Netherlands more effectively. However, King Louis Bonaparte tried to serve Dutch interests instead of his brother's, and he was forced to abdicate on 1 July 1810. The Emperor sent in an army and the Netherlands <a href="/wiki/Incorporation_(Netherlands)" title="Incorporation (Netherlands)">became part of the French Empire</a> until the autumn of 1813 when Napoleon was defeated in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Leipzig" title="Battle of Leipzig">Battle of Leipzig</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_Empire35.PNG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Dutch_Empire35.PNG/220px-Dutch_Empire35.PNG" decoding="async" width="220" height="117" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Dutch_Empire35.PNG/330px-Dutch_Empire35.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Dutch_Empire35.PNG/440px-Dutch_Empire35.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1678" data-file-height="893" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_Empire35.PNG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch colonial empire</a>. Light green: territories administered by or originating from territories administered by the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company" title="Dutch East India Company">Dutch East India Company</a>; dark green: the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_West_India_Company" title="Dutch West India Company">Dutch West India Company</a>. In yellow are the territories occupied later, during the 19th century.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/William_I_of_the_Netherlands" title="William I of the Netherlands">William Frederick</a>, son of the last stadtholder, returned to the Netherlands in 1813 and proclaimed himself <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_Principality_of_the_United_Netherlands" title="Sovereign Principality of the United Netherlands">Sovereign Prince of the Netherlands</a>. Two years later, the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna" title="Congress of Vienna">Congress of Vienna</a> added the southern Netherlands to the north to create a strong country on the northern border of France. William Frederick raised this <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="United Kingdom of the Netherlands">United Netherlands</a> to the status of a kingdom and proclaimed himself as <a href="/wiki/William_I_of_the_Netherlands" title="William I of the Netherlands">King William I</a> in 1815.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In addition, William became hereditary <a href="/wiki/Grand_Ducal_Family_of_Luxembourg" title="Grand Ducal Family of Luxembourg">Grand Duke of Luxembourg</a> in exchange for his German possessions. However, the Southern Netherlands had been culturally separate from the north since 1581, and <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Revolution" title="Belgian Revolution">rebelled</a>. The south gained independence in 1830 as Belgium (recognised by the Northern Netherlands in 1839 as the Kingdom of the Netherlands was created by decree), while the <a href="/wiki/Personal_union" title="Personal union">personal union</a> between Luxembourg and the Netherlands was severed in 1890, when <a href="/wiki/William_III_of_the_Netherlands" title="William III of the Netherlands">William III</a> died with no surviving male heirs. <a href="/wiki/Salic_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Salic Law">Ascendancy laws</a> prevented his daughter <a href="/wiki/Wilhelmina_of_the_Netherlands" title="Wilhelmina of the Netherlands">Queen Wilhelmina</a> from becoming the next Grand Duchess.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Belgian Revolution at home and the <a href="/wiki/Java_War" title="Java War">Java War</a> in the Dutch East Indies brought the Netherlands to the brink of bankruptcy. However, the <a href="/wiki/Cultivation_System" title="Cultivation System">Cultivation System</a> was introduced in 1830; in the Dutch East Indies, 20% of village land had to be devoted to government crops for export. The policy brought the Dutch enormous wealth and made the colony self-sufficient.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands abolished slavery in its colonies in 1863.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Enslaved people in <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">Suriname</a> would be fully free only in 1873, since the law stipulated that there was to be a mandatory 10-year transition.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="World_wars_and_beyond_.281890.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="World_wars_and_beyond_(1890–present)">World wars and beyond (1890–present)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Netherlands_(1900%E2%80%93present)" title="History of the Netherlands (1900–present)">History of the Netherlands (1900–present)</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Netherlands_in_World_War_I" class="mw-redirect" title="The Netherlands in World War I">The Netherlands in World War I</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_in_World_War_II" title="Netherlands in World War II">Netherlands in World War II</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rotterdam,_Laurenskerk,_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg/220px-Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg/330px-Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg/440px-Rotterdam%2C_Laurenskerk%2C_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2269" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rotterdam,_Laurenskerk,_na_bombardement_van_mei_1940.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Rotterdam" title="Rotterdam">Rotterdam</a> after <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam_Blitz" class="mw-redirect" title="Rotterdam Blitz">German air raids</a> in 1940</div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands was able to remain neutral during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, in part because the import of goods through the Netherlands proved essential to German survival until the blockade by the British <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> in 1916.<sup id="cite_ref-Abbenhuis_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Abbenhuis-92">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> That changed in <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, when <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a> <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle of the Netherlands">invaded the Netherlands</a> on 10 May 1940. The <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam_Blitz" class="mw-redirect" title="Rotterdam Blitz">Rotterdam Blitz</a> forced the main element of the Dutch army to surrender four days later. During the occupation, over 100,000 <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_the_Netherlands" title="History of the Jews in the Netherlands">Dutch Jews</a><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> were rounded up and transported to Nazi <a href="/wiki/Extermination_camp" title="Extermination camp">extermination camps</a>; only a few of them survived. Dutch workers were conscripted for forced labour in Germany, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_resistance" title="Dutch resistance">civilians who resisted</a> were killed in reprisal for attacks on German soldiers, and the countryside was plundered for food. Although there were thousands of Dutch who risked their lives by hiding Jews from the Germans, over 20,000 Dutch fascists <a href="/wiki/23rd_SS_Volunteer_Panzer_Grenadier_Division_Nederland" class="mw-redirect" title="23rd SS Volunteer Panzer Grenadier Division Nederland">joined the Waffen SS</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> fighting on the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_II)" title="Eastern Front (World War II)">Eastern Front</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Political <a href="/wiki/Collaboration_with_the_Axis_Powers_during_World_War_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Collaboration with the Axis Powers during World War II">collaborators</a> were members of the <a href="/wiki/Fascism" title="Fascism">fascist</a> <a href="/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_in_the_Netherlands" title="National Socialist Movement in the Netherlands">NSB</a>, the only legal political party in the occupied Netherlands. On 8 December 1941, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_government-in-exile" title="Dutch government-in-exile">Dutch government-in-exile</a> in London declared war on Japan,<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> but could not prevent <a href="/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_Indonesia" class="mw-redirect" title="Japanese occupation of Indonesia">the Japanese occupation of the Dutch East Indies (Indonesia)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> In 1944–45, the <a href="/wiki/First_Canadian_Army" title="First Canadian Army">First Canadian Army</a>, which included Canadian, <a href="/wiki/I_Corps_(United_Kingdom)" title="I Corps (United Kingdom)">British</a> and <a href="/wiki/1st_Armoured_Division_(Poland)" title="1st Armoured Division (Poland)">Polish</a> troops, was responsible for liberating much of the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Soon after <a href="/wiki/Victory_in_Europe_Day" title="Victory in Europe Day">VE Day</a>, the Dutch fought a <a href="/wiki/Indonesian_National_Revolution" title="Indonesian National Revolution">colonial war against the new Republic of Indonesia</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg/220px-Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg/330px-Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg/440px-Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3712" data-file-height="2608" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kok_VanAgt_DeJong_Rutte_Lubbers_Balkenende.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Former Prime Ministers <a href="/wiki/Wim_Kok" title="Wim Kok">Wim Kok</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dries_van_Agt" title="Dries van Agt">Dries van Agt</a>, <a href="/wiki/Piet_de_Jong" title="Piet de Jong">Piet de Jong</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ruud_Lubbers" title="Ruud Lubbers">Ruud Lubbers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jan_Peter_Balkenende" title="Jan Peter Balkenende">Jan Peter Balkenende</a> with Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a>, in 2011</div></div></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Decolonisation">Decolonisation</span></h4>
<p>In 1954, the <a href="/wiki/Charter_for_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Charter for the Kingdom of the Netherlands">Charter for the Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> reformed the political structure of the Netherlands, which was a result of international pressure to carry out <a href="/wiki/Decolonisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Decolonisation">decolonisation</a>. The Dutch colonies of <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(Dutch_colony)" title="Surinam (Dutch colony)">Surinam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies" title="Curaçao and Dependencies">Curaçao and Dependencies</a> and the European country all became countries within the Kingdom, on a basis of equality. Indonesia had declared its independence in August 1945 (recognised in 1949), and thus was never part of the reformed Kingdom.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">Suriname</a> followed in 1975. After the war, the Netherlands left behind an era of neutrality and gained closer ties with neighbouring states. The Netherlands was one of the founding members of <a href="/wiki/Benelux" title="Benelux">Benelux</a> and <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> In the 1950's, the Netherlands became one of the six founding countries of the <a href="/wiki/European_Communities" title="European Communities">European Communities</a>, following the 1952 establishment of the <a href="/wiki/European_Coal_and_Steel_Community" title="European Coal and Steel Community">European Coal and Steel Community</a>, and subsequent 1958 creations of the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Community" title="European Economic Community">European Economic Community</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_Atomic_Energy_Community" class="mw-redirect" title="European Atomic Energy Community">European Atomic Energy Community</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-NL:_EU_member_state_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NL:_EU_member_state-101">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>  In 1993, the former two of these were incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-NL:_EU_member_state_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NL:_EU_member_state-101">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Government-encouraged emigration efforts to reduce <a href="/wiki/Population_density" title="Population density">population density</a> prompted some 500,000 <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch people</a> to leave the country after the war.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The 1960s and 1970s were a time of great social and cultural change, such as rapid de-<a href="/wiki/Pillarisation" title="Pillarisation">pillarisation</a> characterised by the decay of the old divisions along political and religious lines. Students and other youth rejected traditional mores and pushed for change in matters such as <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_rights" title="Women&#39;s rights">women's rights</a>, <a href="/wiki/Human_sexuality" title="Human sexuality">sexuality</a>, <a href="/wiki/Disarmament" title="Disarmament">disarmament</a> and <a href="/wiki/Environmental_issues" title="Environmental issues">environmental issues</a>. In 2002 the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a> was introduced as <a href="/wiki/Fiat_money" title="Fiat money">fiat money</a>, and in 2010 the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> was <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles" title="Dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles">dissolved</a>. Referendums were held on each island to determine their future status. As a result, the islands of <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> (the BES islands) were to obtain closer ties with the Netherlands. This led to the incorporation of these three islands into the country of the Netherlands as <i><a href="/wiki/Special_municipality_(Netherlands)" class="mw-redirect" title="Special municipality (Netherlands)">special municipalities</a></i> upon the dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles. The special municipalities are collectively known as the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_European_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Geography of the European Netherlands">Geography of the European Netherlands</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Low_Countries" title="Low Countries">Low Countries</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg/220px-2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="252" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg/330px-2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg/440px-2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="3440" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2012-NL-prov-relief-3000.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Relief map of the European Netherlands</div></div></div>
<p>The European Netherlands has a total area of 41,543&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (16,040&#160;sq&#160;mi), including water bodies; and a land area of 33,481&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (12,927&#160;sq&#160;mi). The <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a> has a total area of 328&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (127&#160;sq&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> It lies between <a href="/wiki/Latitudes" class="mw-redirect" title="Latitudes">latitudes</a> <a href="/wiki/50th_parallel_north" title="50th parallel north">50°</a> and <a href="/wiki/54th_parallel_north" title="54th parallel north">54° N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/3rd_meridian_east" title="3rd meridian east">3°</a> and <a href="/wiki/8th_meridian_east" title="8th meridian east">8° E</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands is geographically very low relative to sea level and is considered a flat country, with about 26% of its area<sup id="cite_ref-:1_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-29">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> and 21% of its population<sup id="cite_ref-milrek_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-milrek-105">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> located below sea level. The European part of the country is for the most part flat, with the exception of <a href="/wiki/Foothills" title="Foothills">foothills</a> in the far southeast, up to a height of no more than 321 metres, and some low hill ranges in the central parts. Most of the areas below sea level are caused by <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> extraction or achieved through <a href="/wiki/Land_reclamation_in_the_Netherlands" title="Land reclamation in the Netherlands">land reclamation</a>. Since the late 16th century, large <a href="/wiki/Polder" title="Polder">polder</a> areas are preserved through elaborate drainage systems that include <a href="/wiki/Dike_(construction)" class="mw-redirect" title="Dike (construction)">dikes</a>, canals and pumping stations. Nearly 17% of the country's land area is reclaimed from the sea and from lakes.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Much of the country was originally formed by the <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuaries</a> of three large European rivers: the <a href="/wiki/Rhine" title="Rhine">Rhine</a> (<i>Rijn</i>), the <a href="/wiki/Meuse" title="Meuse">Meuse</a> (<i>Maas</i>) and the <a href="/wiki/Scheldt" title="Scheldt">Scheldt</a> (<i>Schelde</i>), as well as their <a href="/wiki/Tributary" title="Tributary">tributaries</a>. The south-western part of the Netherlands is to this day a <a href="/wiki/River_delta" title="River delta">river delta</a> of these three rivers, the <a href="/wiki/Rhine-Meuse-Scheldt_delta" class="mw-redirect" title="Rhine-Meuse-Scheldt delta">Rhine-Meuse-Scheldt delta</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The European Netherlands is divided into north and south parts by the Rhine, the <a href="/wiki/Waal_(river)" title="Waal (river)">Waal</a>, its main tributary branch, and the Meuse. In the past, these rivers functioned as a natural barrier between <a href="/wiki/Fief" title="Fief">fiefdoms</a> and hence historically created a cultural divide, as is evident in some phonetic traits that are recognisable on either side of what the Dutch call their "Great Rivers" (<i>de Grote Rivieren</i>). Another significant branch of the Rhine, the <a href="/wiki/IJssel" title="IJssel">IJssel</a> river, discharges into <a href="/wiki/IJsselmeer" title="IJsselmeer">Lake IJssel</a>, the former <a href="/wiki/Zuiderzee" title="Zuiderzee">Zuiderzee</a> ('southern sea'). Just like the previous, this river forms a linguistic divide: people to the northeast of this river speak <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Low_Saxon" title="Dutch Low Saxon">Dutch Low Saxon</a> dialects (except for the province of <a href="/wiki/Friesland" title="Friesland">Friesland</a>, which has its own language).<sup id="cite_ref-international2000_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-international2000-106">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Geology">Geology</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geology_of_the_Netherlands" title="Geology of the Netherlands">Geology of the Netherlands</a></div>
<p>The modern Netherlands formed as a result of the interplay of the four main rivers (<a href="/wiki/Rhine" title="Rhine">Rhine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Meuse" title="Meuse">Meuse</a>, <a href="/wiki/Schelde" class="mw-redirect" title="Schelde">Schelde</a> and <a href="/wiki/IJssel" title="IJssel">IJssel</a>) and the influence of the <a href="/wiki/North_Sea" title="North Sea">North Sea</a>. The Netherlands is mostly composed of <a href="/wiki/River_delta" title="River delta">deltaic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coast" title="Coast">coastal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Aeolian_processes" title="Aeolian processes">eolian</a> derived sediments during the <a href="/wiki/Pleistocene" title="Pleistocene">Pleistocene</a> <a href="/wiki/Glacial" class="mw-redirect" title="Glacial">glacial</a> and <a href="/wiki/Interglacial" title="Interglacial">interglacial</a> periods.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Almost the entire west Netherlands is composed of the <a href="/wiki/Rhine" title="Rhine">Rhine</a>-<a href="/wiki/Meuse" title="Meuse">Meuse</a> river <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuary</a>, but <a href="/wiki/Civilisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Civilisation">human intervention</a> greatly modified the natural processes at work. Most of the western Netherlands is below sea level due to the human process of turning standing bodies of water into usable land, a <a href="/wiki/Polder" title="Polder">polder</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the east of the Netherlands, remains are found of the <a href="/wiki/Last_Glacial_Period" title="Last Glacial Period">last ice age</a>, which ended approximately ten thousand years ago. As the continental <a href="/wiki/Ice_sheet" title="Ice sheet">ice sheet</a> moved in from the north, it pushed <a href="/wiki/Moraine" title="Moraine">moraine</a> forward. The ice sheet halted as it covered the eastern half of the Netherlands. After the ice age ended, the <a href="/wiki/Moraine" title="Moraine">moraine</a> remained in the form of a long hill-line. The cities of <a href="/wiki/Arnhem" title="Arnhem">Arnhem</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nijmegen" title="Nijmegen">Nijmegen</a> are built upon these hills.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Floods">Floods</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Flood_control_in_the_Netherlands" title="Flood control in the Netherlands">Flood control in the Netherlands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Floods_in_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Floods in the Netherlands">Floods in the Netherlands</a>, <a href="/wiki/North_Sea_flood_of_1953" title="North Sea flood of 1953">North Sea flood of 1953</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Storm_tides_of_the_North_Sea" title="Storm tides of the North Sea">Storm tides of the North Sea</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Christmas_flood_1717.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Christmas_flood_1717.jpg/220px-Christmas_flood_1717.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="135" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Christmas_flood_1717.jpg/330px-Christmas_flood_1717.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Christmas_flood_1717.jpg/440px-Christmas_flood_1717.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3863" data-file-height="2370" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Christmas_flood_1717.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Christmas_flood_of_1717" class="mw-redirect" title="Christmas flood of 1717">Christmas flood of 1717</a> was the result of a northwesterly storm that resulted in the death of thousands.</div></div></div>
<p>Over the centuries, the Dutch coastline has changed considerably as a result of natural disasters and human intervention.
</p><p>On 14 December 1287, <a href="/wiki/St._Lucia%27s_flood" title="St. Lucia&#39;s flood">St. Lucia's flood</a> affected the Netherlands and Germany, killing more than 50,000 people in one of the most destructive floods in recorded history.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/St._Elizabeth%27s_flood_(1421)" title="St. Elizabeth&#39;s flood (1421)">St. Elizabeth flood</a> of 1421 and the mismanagement in its aftermath destroyed a newly reclaimed <a href="/wiki/Polder" title="Polder">polder</a>, replacing it with the 72&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (28&#160;sq&#160;mi) <i><a href="/wiki/Biesbosch" class="mw-redirect" title="Biesbosch">Biesbosch</a></i> tidal floodplains in the south-centre. The huge <a href="/wiki/North_Sea_flood_of_1953" title="North Sea flood of 1953">North Sea flood of February 1953</a> caused the collapse of several dikes in the south-west of the Netherlands; more than 1,800 people drowned in the flood. The Dutch government subsequently instituted a large-scale programme, the "<a href="/wiki/Deltawerken" class="mw-redirect" title="Deltawerken">Delta Works</a>", to protect the country against future flooding, which was completed over a period of more than thirty years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png/220px-The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="229" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png/330px-The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png/440px-The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png 2x" data-file-width="962" data-file-height="1003" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Netherlands_compared_to_sealevel.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map illustrating areas of the Netherlands below sea level</div></div></div>
<p>The impact of disasters was, to an extent, increased through human activity. Relatively high-lying <a href="/wiki/Swamp" title="Swamp">swampland</a> was drained to be used as farmland. The drainage caused the fertile <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> to contract and ground levels to drop, upon which groundwater levels were lowered to compensate for the drop in ground level, causing the underlying peat to contract further. Additionally, until the 19th-century peat was mined, dried, and used for fuel, further exacerbating the problem. Centuries of extensive and poorly controlled <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> extraction lowered an already low land surface by several metres. Even in flooded areas, peat extraction continued through turf dredging.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Because of the flooding, farming was difficult, which encouraged foreign trade, the result of which was that the Dutch were involved in world affairs since the early 14th/15th century.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlands,_Zoetermeer,_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_(1).JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG/220px-Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG/330px-Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG/440px-Netherlands%2C_Zoetermeer%2C_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_%281%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1716" data-file-height="1140" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlands,_Zoetermeer,_Zoetermeerse_Meerpolder_(1).JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A polder at 5.53 metres below sea level</div></div></div>
<p>To guard against floods, a series of defences against the water were contrived. In the first millennium <a href="/wiki/AD" class="mw-redirect" title="AD">AD</a>, villages and farmhouses were built on hills called <i><a href="/wiki/Terp" title="Terp">terps</a></i>. Later, these terps were connected by dikes. In the 12th century, local government agencies called <i>"<a href="/wiki/Water_board_(Netherlands)" title="Water board (Netherlands)">waterschappen</a>"</i> ("water boards") or <i>"<a href="/wiki/Water_board_(Netherlands)" title="Water board (Netherlands)">hoogheemraadschappen</a>"</i> ("high home councils") started to appear, whose job it was to maintain the water level and to protect a region from floods; these agencies continue to exist. As the ground level dropped, the dikes by necessity grew and merged into an integrated system. By the 13th century <a href="/wiki/Windmill" title="Windmill">windmills</a> had come into use to pump water out of areas below sea level. The windmills were later used to drain lakes, creating the famous <a href="/wiki/Polder" title="Polder">polders</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1932 the <i><a href="/wiki/Afsluitdijk" title="Afsluitdijk">Afsluitdijk</a></i> ("Closure Dike") was completed, blocking the former <i><a href="/wiki/Zuiderzee" title="Zuiderzee">Zuiderzee</a></i> (Southern Sea) from the North Sea and thus creating the <a href="/wiki/IJsselmeer" title="IJsselmeer">IJsselmeer</a> (<a href="/wiki/IJssel" title="IJssel">IJssel</a> Lake). It became part of the larger <a href="/wiki/Zuiderzee_Works" title="Zuiderzee Works">Zuiderzee Works</a> in which four polders totalling 2,500 square kilometres (965&#160;sq&#160;mi) were reclaimed from the sea.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands is one of the countries that may suffer most from <a href="/wiki/Climate_change" title="Climate change">climate change</a>. Not only is the rising sea a problem, but erratic weather patterns may cause the rivers to overflow.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Delta_Works">Delta Works</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Delta_Works" title="Delta Works">Delta Works</a> and <a href="/wiki/Flood_control_in_the_Netherlands" title="Flood control in the Netherlands">Flood control in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Deltawerken_na.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Deltawerken_na.png/220px-Deltawerken_na.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="208" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Deltawerken_na.png/330px-Deltawerken_na.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Deltawerken_na.png/440px-Deltawerken_na.png 2x" data-file-width="1622" data-file-height="1532" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Deltawerken_na.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Delta_Works" title="Delta Works">Delta Works</a> are located in the provinces of <a href="/wiki/South_Holland" title="South Holland">South Holland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Zeeland" title="Zeeland">Zeeland</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/North_Sea_Flood_of_1953" class="mw-redirect" title="North Sea Flood of 1953">1953 disaster</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Delta_Works" title="Delta Works">Delta Works</a> was constructed, which is a comprehensive set of civil works throughout the Dutch coast. The project started in 1958 and was largely completed in 1997 with the completion of the <a href="/wiki/Maeslantkering" title="Maeslantkering">Maeslantkering</a>. Since then, new projects have been periodically started to renovate and renew the Delta Works. The main goal of the Delta project was to reduce the risk of flooding in South Holland and Zeeland to once per 10,000 years (compared to once per 4000 years for the rest of the country). This was achieved by raising 3,000&#160;km (1,900&#160;mi) of outer sea-dikes and 10,000&#160;km (6,200&#160;mi) of the inner, canal, and river dikes, and by closing off the sea <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuaries</a> of the Zeeland province. New risk assessments occasionally show problems requiring additional Delta project dike reinforcements. The Delta project is considered by the <a href="/wiki/American_Society_of_Civil_Engineers" title="American Society of Civil Engineers">American Society of Civil Engineers</a> as one of the <a href="/wiki/Wonders_of_the_World#American_Society_of_Civil_Engineers" title="Wonders of the World">seven wonders of the modern world</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is anticipated that <a href="/wiki/Global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Global warming">global warming</a> in the 21st century will result in a rise in <a href="/wiki/Sea_level" title="Sea level">sea level</a>. The Netherlands is actively preparing for a sea-level rise. A politically neutral Delta Commission has formulated an action plan to cope with a sea-level rise of 1.10&#160;m (4&#160;ft) and a simultaneous land height decline of 10&#160;cm (4&#160;in). The plan encompasses the reinforcement of the existing coastal defences like <a href="/wiki/Levee" title="Levee">dikes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dune" title="Dune">dunes</a> with 1.30&#160;m (4.3&#160;ft) of additional flood protection. Climate change will not only threaten the Netherlands from the seaside but could also alter rainfall patterns and river run-off. To protect the country from river flooding, another programme is already being executed. The <a href="/wiki/Room_for_the_River_(Netherlands)" title="Room for the River (Netherlands)">Room for the River plan</a> grants more flow space to rivers, protects the major populated areas and allows for periodic flooding of indefensible lands. The few residents who lived in these so-called "overflow areas" have been moved to higher ground, with some of that ground having been raised above anticipated flood levels.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT021313_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT021313-117">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate_change">Climate change</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands" title="Climate change in the Netherlands">Climate change in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><div class="excerpt">
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Co-emissions-per-capita_(2).png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png/220px-Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png/330px-Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png/440px-Co-emissions-per-capita_%282%29.png 2x" data-file-width="3400" data-file-height="2400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Co-emissions-per-capita_(2).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>CO<sub>2</sub> emissions per capita in the European Union. (<a href="/wiki/Our_World_in_Data" title="Our World in Data">Our World in Data</a>)</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands" title="Climate change in the Netherlands">Climate change in the Netherlands</a> is already affecting the country. The average temperature in the Netherlands rose by more than 2&#160;°C from 1901 to 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Climate change has resulted in increased frequency of droughts and heatwaves. Because significant portions of the Netherlands have been <a href="/wiki/Land_reclamation_in_the_Netherlands" title="Land reclamation in the Netherlands">reclaimed from the sea</a> or otherwise are very near sea level, the Netherlands is very vulnerable to <a href="/wiki/Sea_level_rise" title="Sea level rise">sea level rise</a>.
</p>
The Netherlands has the fourth largest <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions_per_capita" title="List of countries by carbon dioxide emissions per capita">CO<sub>2</sub> emissions per capita</a> of the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:3_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:3-119">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>  The Dutch government has set goals to lower emissions in the next few decades. The Dutch response to climate change is driven by a number of unique factors, including larger <a href="/wiki/Green_recovery" title="Green recovery">green recovery</a> plans by the European Union in the face of the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Netherlands" title="COVID-19 pandemic in the Netherlands">COVID-19</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_litigation" title="Climate change litigation">climate change litigation</a> case, <a href="/wiki/State_of_the_Netherlands_v._Urgenda_Foundation" title="State of the Netherlands v. Urgenda Foundation">State of the Netherlands v. Urgenda Foundation</a>, which created mandatory <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_mitigation" title="Climate change mitigation">climate change mitigation</a> through emissions reductions 25% below 1990 levels.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_BBC_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_BBC-120">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_AP_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_AP-121">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> At the end of 2018 <a href="/wiki/Greenhouse_gas" title="Greenhouse gas">CO<sub>2</sub> emissions</a> were down 15% compared to 1990 levels.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:4_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:4-122">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The goal of the Dutch government is to reduce emissions in 2030 by 49%.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:6_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_the_Netherlands_:6-123">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nature">Nature</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_the_Netherlands" title="List of national parks of the Netherlands">List of national parks of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_extinct_animals_of_the_Netherlands" title="List of extinct animals of the Netherlands">List of extinct animals of the Netherlands</a></div>
<p>The Netherlands has 21 national parks<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> and hundreds of other nature reserves, that include <a href="/wiki/Lake" title="Lake">lakes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Heathland" class="mw-redirect" title="Heathland">heathland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Woodland" title="Woodland">woods</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dune" title="Dune">dunes</a>, and other habitats. Most of these are owned by <a href="/wiki/Staatsbosbeheer" title="Staatsbosbeheer">Staatsbosbeheer</a>, the national department for <a href="/wiki/Forestry" title="Forestry">forestry</a> and <a href="/wiki/Habitat_conservation" title="Habitat conservation">nature conservation</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vereniging_Natuurmonumenten" title="Vereniging Natuurmonumenten">Natuurmonumenten</a> (literally 'Natures monuments'), a private organisation that buys, protects and manages nature reserves.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The <a href="/wiki/Wadden_Sea" title="Wadden Sea">Wadden Sea</a> in the north, with its <a href="/wiki/Tidal_flat" class="mw-redirect" title="Tidal flat">tidal flats</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wetland" title="Wetland">wetlands</a>, is rich in <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity" title="Biodiversity">biological diversity</a>, and is a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Nature Site</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg/220px-Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg/330px-Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg/440px-Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2856" data-file-height="1707" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Phoca_vitulina_Terschelling.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Harbor_seal" title="Harbor seal">Common seals</a> on <a href="/wiki/Terschelling" title="Terschelling">Terschelling</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Wadden_Sea" title="Wadden Sea">Wadden Sea</a> island</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Oosterschelde" class="mw-redirect" title="Oosterschelde">Oosterschelde</a>, formerly the northeast <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuary</a> of the river <a href="/wiki/Scheldt" title="Scheldt">Scheldt</a> was designated a national park in 2002, thereby making it the largest national park in the Netherlands at an area of 370&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (140&#160;sq&#160;mi). It consists primarily of the salt waters of the Oosterschelde but also includes mudflats, meadows, and shoals. Because of the large variety of sea life, including unique regional species, the park is popular with <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">Scuba divers</a>. Other activities include sailing, fishing, cycling, and bird watching.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Phytogeography" title="Phytogeography">Phytogeographically</a>, the European Netherlands is shared between the Atlantic European and Central European provinces of the <a href="/wiki/Circumboreal_Region" title="Circumboreal Region">Circumboreal Region</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom" title="Boreal Kingdom">Boreal Kingdom</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature" title="World Wide Fund for Nature">World Wide Fund for Nature</a>, the European territory of the Netherlands belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Ecoregion" title="Ecoregion">ecoregion</a> of <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_mixed_forests" title="Atlantic mixed forests">Atlantic mixed forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-126">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> In 1871, the last old original natural woods were cut down,<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> and most woods today are planted monocultures of trees like <a href="/wiki/Scots_pine" class="mw-redirect" title="Scots pine">Scots pine</a> and trees that are not native to the Netherlands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2009)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> These woods were planted on <a href="/wiki/Heath_(habitat)#Anthropogenic_heaths" class="mw-redirect" title="Heath (habitat)">anthropogenic heaths</a> and sand-drifts (overgrazed heaths) (<a href="/wiki/Veluwe" title="Veluwe">Veluwe</a>). The Netherlands had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 0.6/10, ranking it 169th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-128">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The number of flying insects in the Netherlands has dropped by 75% since the 1990s.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Caribbean_islands">Caribbean islands</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba (island)</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Antilles" title="Lesser Antilles">Lesser Antilles</a> islands of the Caribbean, the territories of <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a> have a constituent country status within the wider Kingdom of the Netherlands. Another three territories which make up the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a> are designated as special municipalities of the Netherlands. The Caribbean Netherlands have maritime borders with <a href="/wiki/Anguilla" title="Anguilla">Anguilla</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>, <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> (<a href="/wiki/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Saint Barthélemy">Saint Barthélemy</a>), <a href="/wiki/Saint_Kitts_and_Nevis" title="Saint Kitts and Nevis">Saint Kitts and Nevis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Virgin_Islands" title="United States Virgin Islands">U.S. Virgin Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Caribbean-Borders_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Caribbean-Borders-130">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The islands of the Caribbean Netherlands enjoy a <a href="/wiki/Tropical" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical">tropical</a> <a href="/wiki/Climate" title="Climate">climate</a> with warm weather all year round.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:282px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life(js).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg/280px-Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="280" height="130" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg/420px-Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg/560px-Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life%28js%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="10000" data-file-height="4636" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kleine_Bonaire-Underwater_life(js).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Underwater life of <a href="/wiki/Klein_Bonaire" title="Klein Bonaire">Klein Bonaire</a></div></div></div>
<p>Within this island group:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a> is part of the <a href="/wiki/ABC_islands_(Lesser_Antilles)" class="mw-redirect" title="ABC islands (Lesser Antilles)">ABC islands</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Antilles" title="Leeward Antilles">Leeward Antilles</a> island chain off the <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuelan</a> coast. The Leeward Antilles have a mixed volcanic and coral origin.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a> are part of the <a href="/wiki/SSS_islands" title="SSS islands">SSS islands</a>. They are located east of <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_Islands" title="Virgin Islands">Virgin Islands</a>. Although in the English language they are considered part of the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands" title="Leeward Islands">Leeward Islands</a>, French, Spanish, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> and the English spoken locally consider them part of the <a href="/wiki/Windward_Islands" title="Windward Islands">Windward Islands</a>. The Windward Islands are all of volcanic origin and hilly, leaving little ground suitable for <a href="/wiki/Agriculture" title="Agriculture">agriculture</a>. The highest point is <a href="/wiki/Mount_Scenery" title="Mount Scenery">Mount Scenery</a>, 887&#160;m (2,910&#160;ft), on <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a>. This is the highest point in the country and is also the highest point of the entire <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Netherlands" title="Politics of the Netherlands">Politics of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg/220px-Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg/330px-Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg/440px-Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1365" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Den_Haag_-_panoramio_-_Nikolai_Karaneschev.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Binnenhof" title="Binnenhof">Binnenhof</a>, where the lower and upper houses of the States General meet</div></div></div>
<p>he Netherlands has been a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy" title="Constitutional monarchy">constitutional monarchy</a> since 1815, and due to the efforts of <a href="/wiki/Johan_Rudolph_Thorbecke" title="Johan Rudolph Thorbecke">Johan Rudolph Thorbecke</a><sup id="cite_ref-NRC_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NRC-132">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> became a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary democracy</a> in 1848. The Netherlands is described as a <a href="/wiki/Consociational_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Consociational state">consociational state</a>. Dutch politics and governance are characterised by an effort to achieve broad consensus on important issues, within both the political community and society as a whole. In 2017, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist">The Economist</a></i> ranked the Netherlands as the 11th <a href="/wiki/Democracy_Index" title="Democracy Index">most democratic country in the world</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Dutch_monarchy" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch monarchy">monarch</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>, at present King <a href="/wiki/Willem-Alexander_of_the_Netherlands" title="Willem-Alexander of the Netherlands">Willem-Alexander of the Netherlands</a>. Constitutionally, the position is equipped with limited powers. By law, the King has the right to be periodically briefed and consulted on government affairs. Depending on the personalities and relationships of the King and the ministers, the monarch might have influence beyond the power granted by the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Netherlands" title="Constitution of the Netherlands">Constitution of the Netherlands</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:310px;max-width:310px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:154px;max-width:154px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Willem-Alexander_(Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm,_2010)_cropped.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg/152px-Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg" decoding="async" width="152" height="222" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg/228px-Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg/304px-Willem-Alexander_%28Royal_Wedding_in_Stockholm%2C_2010%29_cropped.jpg 2x" data-file-width="789" data-file-height="1152" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Willem-Alexander_of_the_Netherlands" title="Willem-Alexander of the Netherlands">Willem-Alexander</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Monarchy of the Netherlands">King</a> <br />since 2013</small></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:152px;max-width:152px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Mark_Rutte-6_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg/150px-Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="223" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg/225px-Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg/300px-Mark_Rutte-6_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2105" data-file-height="3131" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a> <br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_Netherlands" title="Prime Minister of the Netherlands">Prime Minister</a><br />since 2010</small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive power</a> is formed by the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_(Netherlands)" title="Council of Ministers (Netherlands)">Council of Ministers</a>, the deliberative organ of the <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_the_Netherlands" title="Cabinet of the Netherlands">Dutch cabinet</a>. The cabinet usually consists of 13 to 16 ministers and a varying number of <a href="/wiki/State_Secretary_(Netherlands)" class="mw-redirect" title="State Secretary (Netherlands)">state secretaries</a>. One to three ministers are <a href="/wiki/Minister_without_portfolio" title="Minister without portfolio">ministers without portfolio</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_Netherlands" title="Prime Minister of the Netherlands">Prime Minister of the Netherlands</a>, who often is the leader of the largest party of the coalition. The Prime Minister is a <i><a href="/wiki/Primus_inter_pares" title="Primus inter pares">primus inter pares</a></i>, with no explicit powers beyond those of the other ministers. <a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a> has been Prime Minister since October 2010; the Prime Minister had been the leader of the largest party of the governing coalition continuously since 1973.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The cabinet is <a href="/wiki/Ministerial_responsibility" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministerial responsibility">responsible</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Bicameralism" title="Bicameralism">bicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliament" title="Parliament">parliament</a>, the <a href="/wiki/States_General_of_the_Netherlands" title="States General of the Netherlands">States General</a>, which also has <a href="/wiki/Legislative" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative">legislative powers</a>. The 150 members of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_(Netherlands)" title="House of Representatives (Netherlands)">House of Representatives</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Lower_house" title="Lower house">lower house</a>, are elected in <a href="/wiki/Direct_election" title="Direct election">direct elections</a> on the basis of <a href="/wiki/Party-list_proportional_representation" title="Party-list proportional representation">party-list proportional representation</a>. These are held every four years, or sooner in case the cabinet falls (for example: when one of the chambers carries a <a href="/wiki/Motion_of_no_confidence" title="Motion of no confidence">motion of no confidence</a>, the cabinet offers its resignation to the monarch). The provincial assemblies, the <a href="/wiki/States-Provincial" class="mw-redirect" title="States-Provincial">States Provincial</a>, are directly elected every four years as well. The members of the provincial assemblies elect the 75 members of the <a href="/wiki/Senate_(Netherlands)" title="Senate (Netherlands)">Senate</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Upper_house" title="Upper house">upper house</a>, which has the power to reject laws, but not propose or amend them.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_culture">Political culture</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:541px;max-width:541px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:185px;max-width:185px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:120px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG/183px-Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG" decoding="async" width="183" height="120" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG/275px-Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG/366px-Red-light_district_of_Amsterdam_by_day._2012.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4280" data-file-height="2811" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/De_Wallen" title="De Wallen">De Wallen</a>, Amsterdam's red-light district offers activities such as legal <a href="/wiki/Prostitution_in_the_Netherlands" title="Prostitution in the Netherlands">prostitution</a>, symbolizing the Dutch political culture and tradition of tolerance.</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:181px;max-width:181px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:120px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Weddinginholland.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Two men marrying in Amsterdam" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Weddinginholland.jpg/179px-Weddinginholland.jpg" decoding="async" width="179" height="120" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Weddinginholland.jpg/269px-Weddinginholland.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Weddinginholland.jpg/358px-Weddinginholland.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1781" data-file-height="1196" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">The Netherlands is known for its progressive attitude towards <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Netherlands" title="LGBT rights in the Netherlands">LGBT rights</a>.
