Here we can see two men marrying in 2001, one month after the Netherlands became the first country to legalize such marriages.</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:169px;max-width:169px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:120px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg" class="image"><img alt="A Dutch Coffee shop which sells marijuana" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg/167px-CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg" decoding="async" width="167" height="120" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg/251px-CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg/334px-CoffeeShopAmsterdam.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2320" data-file-height="1672" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Many <a href="/wiki/Recreational_drugs" class="mw-redirect" title="Recreational drugs">recreational drugs</a> are legal in the Netherlands, thanks to its progressive <a href="/wiki/Drug_policy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Drug policy of the Netherlands">drug policy</a>. <a href="/wiki/Coffeeshop_(Netherlands)" title="Coffeeshop (Netherlands)">Coffeeshops</a>, the Dutch term for a store which sells <a href="/wiki/Cannabis_(drug)" title="Cannabis (drug)">marijuana</a>, can be found across the country.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Both trade unions and <a href="/wiki/Employers_organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Employers organisation">employers organisations</a> are consulted beforehand in policymaking in the financial, economic and social areas. They meet regularly with the government in the <a href="/wiki/Social-Economic_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Social-Economic Council">Social-Economic Council</a>. This body advises government and its advice cannot be put aside easily.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands has a tradition of <a href="/wiki/Toleration" title="Toleration">social tolerance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In the 18th century, while the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Reformed_Church" title="Dutch Reformed Church">Dutch Reformed Church</a> was the <a href="/wiki/State_religion" title="State religion">state religion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism in the Netherlands">Catholicism</a>, other forms of Protestantism, such as Baptists and Lutherans, as well as <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_the_Netherlands" title="History of the Jews in the Netherlands">Judaism</a> were tolerated but discriminated against.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the late 19th century this Dutch tradition of religious tolerance transformed into a system of <a href="/wiki/Pillarisation" title="Pillarisation">pillarisation</a>, in which religious groups coexisted separately and only interacted at the level of government.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> This tradition of tolerance influences Dutch <a href="/wiki/Criminal_justice_system_of_the_Netherlands" title="Criminal justice system of the Netherlands">criminal justice</a> policies on <a href="/wiki/Drug_policy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Drug policy of the Netherlands">recreational drugs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prostitution_in_the_Netherlands" title="Prostitution in the Netherlands">prostitution</a>, <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Netherlands" title="LGBT rights in the Netherlands">LGBT rights</a>, <a href="/wiki/Euthanasia" title="Euthanasia">euthanasia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Abortion_in_the_Netherlands" title="Abortion in the Netherlands">abortion</a>, which are among the most liberal in the world.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_parties">Political parties</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Political_parties_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Political parties of the Netherlands">Political parties of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Netherlands" title="Politics of the Netherlands">Politics of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ineke_van_Gent,_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven,_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg/220px-Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg/330px-Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg/440px-Ineke_van_Gent%2C_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven%2C_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5616" data-file-height="3744" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ineke_van_Gent,_Han_ten_Broeke_en_Kees_Verhoeven,_Lancering_Europese_Mobiliteitsweek_2010.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Netherlands has a culture of respectful and friendly debate. From left to right, members of the House of Representatives <a href="/wiki/Sander_de_Rouwe" title="Sander de Rouwe">Sander de Rouwe</a> (CDA), <a href="/wiki/Ineke_van_Gent" title="Ineke van Gent">Ineke van Gent</a> (GL), <a href="/wiki/Han_ten_Broeke" title="Han ten Broeke">Han ten Broeke</a> (VVD), <a href="/wiki/Kees_Verhoeven" title="Kees Verhoeven">Kees Verhoeven</a> (D66) and <a href="/wiki/Farshad_Bashir" title="Farshad Bashir">Farshad Bashir</a> (SP), 2010</div></div></div>
<p>No single party has held a majority in parliament since the 19th century, and as a result, <a href="/wiki/Coalition_government" title="Coalition government">coalition cabinets</a> had to be formed. Since suffrage became universal <a href="/wiki/Pacification_of_1917" title="Pacification of 1917">in 1917</a>, the Dutch political system has been dominated by three families of political parties: <a href="/wiki/Christian_democracy" title="Christian democracy">Christian Democrats</a> (currently the <a href="/wiki/Christian_Democratic_Appeal" title="Christian Democratic Appeal">CDA</a>), <a href="/wiki/Social_democracy" title="Social democracy">Social Democrats</a> (currently the <a href="/wiki/Labour_Party_(Netherlands)" title="Labour Party (Netherlands)">PvdA</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Liberalism_in_the_Netherlands" title="Liberalism in the Netherlands">Liberals</a> (currently the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Party_for_Freedom_and_Democracy" title="People&#39;s Party for Freedom and Democracy">VVD</a>).
</p><p>These parties co-operated in coalition cabinets in which the Christian Democrats had always been a partner: so either a centre-left coalition of the Christian Democrats and Social Democrats was ruling or a centre-right coalition of Christian Democrats and Liberals. In the 1970s, the <a href="/wiki/Party_system" title="Party system">party system</a> became more volatile: the Christian Democratic parties lost seats, while new parties became successful, such as the <a href="/wiki/Radicalism_(historical)" class="mw-redirect" title="Radicalism (historical)">radical democrat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Progressivism" title="Progressivism">progressive</a> liberal <a href="/wiki/Democrats_66" title="Democrats 66">Democrats 66</a> (D66) or the <a href="/wiki/Ecology" title="Ecology">ecologist</a> party <a href="/wiki/GroenLinks" title="GroenLinks">GroenLinks</a> (GL).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_1994" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 1994">1994 election</a>, the CDA lost its dominant position. A "<a href="/wiki/Purple_(government)" class="mw-redirect" title="Purple (government)">purple</a>" cabinet was formed by the VVD, D66, and PvdA. In the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2002" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2002">2002 elections</a>, this cabinet lost its majority, because of an increased support for the CDA and the rise of the right-wing <a href="/wiki/Lijst_Pim_Fortuyn" class="mw-redirect" title="Lijst Pim Fortuyn">LPF</a>, a new political party, around <a href="/wiki/Pim_Fortuyn" title="Pim Fortuyn">Pim Fortuyn</a>, who was assassinated a week before the elections. A short-lived <a href="/wiki/First_Balkenende_cabinet" title="First Balkenende cabinet">cabinet</a> was formed by CDA, VVD, and LPF, which was led by the CDA Leader <a href="/wiki/Jan_Peter_Balkenende" title="Jan Peter Balkenende">Jan Peter Balkenende</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2003" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2003">2003 elections</a>, in which the LPF lost most of its seats, a <a href="/wiki/Balkenende_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Balkenende II">cabinet</a> was formed by the CDA, VVD, and D66. The cabinet initiated an ambitious programme of reforming the <a href="/wiki/Welfare_state" title="Welfare state">welfare state</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_the_Netherlands" title="Healthcare in the Netherlands">healthcare system</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Border_control#Immigration_law_and_policy" title="Border control">immigration policy</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In June 2006, the cabinet fell after D66 voted in favour of a motion of no confidence against the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_and_Security_(Netherlands)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Justice and Security (Netherlands)">Minister of Immigration and Integration</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rita_Verdonk" title="Rita Verdonk">Rita Verdonk</a>, who had instigated an investigation of the asylum procedure of <a href="/wiki/Ayaan_Hirsi_Ali" title="Ayaan Hirsi Ali">Ayaan Hirsi Ali</a>, a VVD <a href="/wiki/Member_of_parliament" title="Member of parliament">MP</a>. A <a href="/wiki/Third_Balkenende_cabinet" title="Third Balkenende cabinet">caretaker cabinet</a> was formed by the CDA and VVD, and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2006">general elections</a> were held on 22 November 2006. In these elections, the CDA remained the largest party and the <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Party_(Netherlands)" title="Socialist Party (Netherlands)">Socialist Party</a> made the largest gains. The <a href="/wiki/2006%E2%80%9307_Dutch_cabinet_formation" class="mw-redirect" title="2006–07 Dutch cabinet formation">formation of a new cabinet</a> took three months, resulting in a <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_cabinet_Balkenende-4" class="mw-redirect" title="Netherlands cabinet Balkenende-4">coalition</a> of CDA, PvdA, and <a href="/wiki/Christian_Union_(Netherlands)" title="Christian Union (Netherlands)">Christian Union</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 20 February 2010, the cabinet fell when the PvdA refused to prolong the involvement of the Dutch Army in <a href="/wiki/Uruzgan" class="mw-redirect" title="Uruzgan">Uruzgan</a>, Afghanistan.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Snap_election" title="Snap election">Snap elections</a> were held on <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2010" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2010">9 June 2010</a>, with devastating results for the previously largest party, the CDA, which lost about half of its seats, resulting in 21 seats. The VVD became the largest party with 31 seats, closely followed by the PvdA with 30 seats. The big winner of the 2010 elections was <a href="/wiki/Geert_Wilders" title="Geert Wilders">Geert Wilders</a>, whose right wing <a href="/wiki/Party_for_Freedom" title="Party for Freedom">PVV</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> the ideological successor to the <a href="/wiki/Pim_Fortuyn_List" title="Pim Fortuyn List">LPF</a>, more than doubled its number of seats.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/2010_Dutch_cabinet_formation" title="2010 Dutch cabinet formation">Negotiation talks for a new government</a> resulted in a <a href="/wiki/Minority_government" title="Minority government">minority government</a>, led by VVD (a first) in coalition with CDA, which was sworn in on 14 October 2010. This unprecedented minority government was supported by PVV, but proved ultimately to be unstable,<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> when on 21 April 2012, Wilders, leader of PVV, unexpectedly 'torpedoed seven weeks of austerity talks' on new austerity measures, paving the way for early elections.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>VVD and PvdA won a majority in the House of Representatives during the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2012" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2012">2012 general election</a>. On 5 November 2012 they formed the <a href="/wiki/Second_Rutte_cabinet" title="Second Rutte cabinet">second Rutte cabinet</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2017" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch general election, 2017">2017 general election</a>, <a href="/wiki/VVD" class="mw-redirect" title="VVD">VVD</a>, <a href="/wiki/Christian_Democratic_Appeal" title="Christian Democratic Appeal">Christian Democratic Appeal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Democrats_66" title="Democrats 66">Democrats 66</a> and <a href="/wiki/ChristenUnie" class="mw-redirect" title="ChristenUnie">ChristenUnie</a> formed the <a href="/wiki/Third_Rutte_cabinet" title="Third Rutte cabinet">third Rutte cabinet</a>. This cabinet resigned in January 2021, two months before the general election, after a <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_child_welfare_fraud_scandal" class="mw-redirect" title="Netherlands child welfare fraud scandal">child welfare fraud scandal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> In March 2021, centre-right VVD of Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a> was the winner of the <a href="/wiki/2021_Netherlands_general_election" class="mw-redirect" title="2021 Netherlands general election">elections</a>, securing 34 out of 150 seats. The second biggest party was the centre-left D66 with 24 seats. <a href="/wiki/Geert_Wilders" title="Geert Wilders">Geert Wilders</a>' far-right party lost support. Prime Minister Mark Rutte, in power since 2010, formed his fourth coalition government, the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Rutte_cabinet" title="Fourth Rutte cabinet">Fourth Rutte cabinet</a>, consisting of the same parties as the previous one.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Netherlands" title="Administrative divisions of the Netherlands">Administrative divisions of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Public_body_(Netherlands)" title="Public body (Netherlands)">Public body (Netherlands)</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg/200px-Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="237" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg/300px-Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg/400px-Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="743" data-file-height="881" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_provinces_Netherlands-en.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_the_Netherlands" title="Provinces of the Netherlands">Provinces</a> and <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands#Administration" title="Caribbean Netherlands">territories</a> of the Netherlands</div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands is divided into twelve provinces, each under a <a href="/wiki/King%27s_Commissioner" class="mw-redirect" title="King&#39;s Commissioner">King's Commissioner</a> (<i>Commissaris van de Koning</i>). Informally in <a href="/wiki/Limburg_(Netherlands)" title="Limburg (Netherlands)">Limburg province</a> this position is named Governor (<i>Gouverneur</i>).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> All provinces are divided into <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_the_Netherlands" title="Municipalities of the Netherlands">municipalities</a> (<i>gemeenten</i>), of which there are 342 (2023).<sup id="cite_ref-CBS_code_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CBS_code-146">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country is also subdivided into 21 water districts, governed by a <a href="/wiki/Water_board_(Netherlands)" title="Water board (Netherlands)">water board</a> (<i>waterschap</i> or <i>hoogheemraadschap</i>), each having authority in matters concerning water management.<sup id="cite_ref-WATER_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WATER-147">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The creation of water boards actually pre-dates that of the nation itself, the first appearing in 1196. The Dutch water boards are among the oldest democratic entities in the world still in existence. Direct elections of the water boards take place every four years.
</p><p>The administrative structure on the three BES islands, collectively known as the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a>, is outside the twelve provinces. These islands have the status of <i>openbare lichamen (<a href="/wiki/Public_body_(Netherlands)" title="Public body (Netherlands)">public bodies</a>)</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-WOLBES_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WOLBES-149">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> In the Netherlands these administrative units are often referred to as <i>special municipalities</i>.
</p><p>Within the Dutch town of <a href="/wiki/Baarle-Nassau" title="Baarle-Nassau">Baarle-Nassau</a>, are 22 Belgian <a href="/wiki/Enclave_and_exclave" title="Enclave and exclave">exclaves</a><sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> and within those are 8 Dutch <a href="/wiki/Enclave_and_exclave" title="Enclave and exclave">enclaves</a>.
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Netherlands" title="Foreign relations of the Netherlands">Foreign relations of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_the_Netherlands" title="List of diplomatic missions of the Netherlands">List of diplomatic missions of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:International_Court_of_Justice.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/International_Court_of_Justice.jpg/220px-International_Court_of_Justice.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/International_Court_of_Justice.jpg/330px-International_Court_of_Justice.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/International_Court_of_Justice.jpg/440px-International_Court_of_Justice.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="597" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:International_Court_of_Justice.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Peace_Palace" title="Peace Palace">Peace Palace</a> (<i>Vredespaleis</i>), in <a href="/wiki/The_Hague" title="The Hague">The Hague</a></div></div></div>
<p>The history of <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Netherlands" title="Foreign relations of the Netherlands">Dutch foreign policy</a> has been characterised by its <a href="/wiki/Neutral_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Neutral state">neutrality</a>. Since World War II, the Netherlands has become a member of a large number of international organisations, most prominently the UN, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> and the EU. The Dutch economy is very open and relies strongly on <a href="/wiki/International_trade" title="International trade">international trade</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Foreign_policy" title="Foreign policy">foreign policy</a> of the Netherlands is based on four basic commitments: to <a href="/wiki/Atlanticism" title="Atlanticism">Atlantic co-operation</a>, to <a href="/wiki/European_integration" title="European integration">European integration</a>, to <a href="/wiki/International_development" title="International development">international development</a> and to <a href="/wiki/International_law" title="International law">international law</a>. One of the more controversial international issues surrounding the Netherlands is its <a href="/wiki/Drug_policy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Drug policy of the Netherlands">liberal policy towards soft drugs</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The historical ties inherited from its colonial past in <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">Surinam</a> still influence the foreign relations of the Netherlands. In addition, many people from these countries are living permanently in the Netherlands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Armed_forces_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Armed forces of the Netherlands">Armed forces of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg/220px-Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg/330px-Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg/440px-Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Portretfoto_Onno_Eichelsheim.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>General <a href="/wiki/Onno_Eichelsheim" title="Onno Eichelsheim">Onno Eichelsheim</a> is the current <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_Defence_(Netherlands)" title="Chief of Defence (Netherlands)">Chief of Defence</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands has one of the oldest standing armies in Europe; it was first established as such by <a href="/wiki/Maurice_of_Nassau" class="mw-redirect" title="Maurice of Nassau">Maurice of Nassau</a> in the late 1500s. The Dutch army was used throughout the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch Empire</a>. After the defeat of Napoleon, the Dutch army was transformed into a <a href="/wiki/Conscription" title="Conscription">conscription</a> army. The army was unsuccessfully deployed during the <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Revolution" title="Belgian Revolution">Belgian Revolution</a> in 1830. After 1830, it was deployed mainly in the Dutch colonies, as the Netherlands remained neutral in European wars (including the First World War), until the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle of the Netherlands">Netherlands was invaded in World War II</a> and defeated by the Wehrmacht in May 1940.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:HNLMS_Holland.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/HNLMS_Holland.jpg/220px-HNLMS_Holland.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/HNLMS_Holland.jpg/330px-HNLMS_Holland.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/HNLMS_Holland.jpg/440px-HNLMS_Holland.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:HNLMS_Holland.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Zr. Ms. Holland</i>, a <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a> <a href="/wiki/Offshore_patrol_vessel" class="mw-redirect" title="Offshore patrol vessel">offshore patrol vessel</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands abandoned its neutrality in 1948 when it signed the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Brussels" title="Treaty of Brussels">Treaty of Brussels</a>, and became a founding member of <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> in 1949. The Dutch military was therefore part of the NATO strength in <a href="/wiki/Cold_War" title="Cold War">Cold War</a> Europe, deploying its army to several bases in Germany. More than 3,000 Dutch soldiers were assigned to the <a href="/wiki/2nd_Infantry_Division_(United_States)" title="2nd Infantry Division (United States)">2nd Infantry Division</a> of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Army" title="United States Army">United States Army</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Korean_War" title="Korean War">Korean War</a>. In 1996 conscription was suspended, and the Dutch army was once again transformed into a professional army. Since the 1990s the Dutch army has been involved in the <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_War" title="Bosnian War">Bosnian War</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_War" title="Kosovo War">Kosovo War</a>, it held a province in <a href="/wiki/Iraq" title="Iraq">Iraq</a> after the defeat of <a href="/wiki/Saddam_Hussein" title="Saddam Hussein">Saddam Hussein</a>, and it was engaged in <a href="/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_(2001%E2%80%93present)" class="mw-redirect" title="War in Afghanistan (2001–present)">Afghanistan</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Netherlands has ratified many international conventions concerning <a href="/wiki/International_humanitarian_law" title="International humanitarian law">war law</a>. The Netherlands decided not to sign the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The military is composed of four branches, all of which carry the prefix <i>Koninklijke</i> (Royal):
</p>
<ul><li><i>Koninklijke Marine</i> (KM), the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a>, including the Naval Air Service and Marine Corps;</li>
<li><i>Koninklijke Landmacht</i> (KL), the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Army" title="Royal Netherlands Army">Royal Netherlands Army</a>;</li>
<li><i>Koninklijke Luchtmacht</i> (KLu), the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Air_Force" title="Royal Netherlands Air Force">Royal Netherlands Air Force</a>;</li>
<li><i>Koninklijke Marechaussee</i> (KMar), the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Marechaussee" title="Royal Marechaussee">Royal Marechaussee</a> (Military Police), tasks include military police and border control.</li></ul>
<p>The submarine service opened to women on 1 January 2017. The <a href="/wiki/Korps_Commandotroepen" title="Korps Commandotroepen">Korps Commandotroepen</a>, the Special Operations Force of the Netherlands Army, is open to women, but because of the extremely high physical demands for initial training, it is almost impossible for a woman to become a commando.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch Ministry of Defence employs more than 70,000 personnel, including over 20,000 civilians and over 50,000 military personnel.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Economy of the Netherlands">Economy of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg/220px-Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg/330px-Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg/440px-Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1026" data-file-height="648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Economic_growth_of_the_Netherlands.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historical GDP per capita development (<a href="/wiki/Our_World_in_Data" title="Our World in Data">Our World in Data</a>)</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Netherlands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Netherlands exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands has a developed economy and has been playing a special role in the European economy for many centuries. Since the 16th century, shipping, fishing, agriculture, trade, and banking have been leading sectors of the Dutch economy. The Netherlands has <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_economic_freedom" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by economic freedom">a high level of economic freedom</a>. The Netherlands is one of the top countries in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Enabling_Trade_Report" title="Global Enabling Trade Report">Global Enabling Trade Report</a> (2nd in 2016), and was ranked the fifth most competitive economy in the world by the Swiss <a href="/wiki/International_Institute_for_Management_Development" title="International Institute for Management Development">International Institute for Management Development</a> in 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the country was ranked the 5th most innovative nation in the world in the 2022 <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> down from 2nd in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Netherlands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the key trading partners of the Netherlands were Germany, Belgium, the United Kingdom, the United States, France, Italy, China and Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> The Netherlands is one of the world's 10 leading exporting countries. Foodstuffs form the largest industrial sector. Other major industries include chemicals, metallurgy, machinery, electrical goods, trade, services and tourism. Examples of international Dutch companies operating in Netherlands include <a href="/wiki/Randstad_Holding" class="mw-redirect" title="Randstad Holding">Randstad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Heineken_International" class="mw-redirect" title="Heineken International">Heineken</a>, <a href="/wiki/KLM" title="KLM">KLM</a>, financial services (<a href="/wiki/ING_Group" title="ING Group">ING</a>, <a href="/wiki/ABN_AMRO" title="ABN AMRO">ABN AMRO</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rabobank" title="Rabobank">Rabobank</a>), chemicals (<a href="/wiki/DSM_(company)" title="DSM (company)">DSM</a>, <a href="/wiki/AkzoNobel" title="AkzoNobel">AKZO</a>), petroleum refining (<a href="/wiki/Royal_Dutch_Shell" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Dutch Shell">Royal Dutch Shell</a>), electronic machinery (<a href="/wiki/Philips" title="Philips">Philips</a>, <a href="/wiki/ASML_Holding" title="ASML Holding">ASML</a>), and satellite navigation (<a href="/wiki/TomTom" title="TomTom">TomTom</a>).
</p><p>The Netherlands has the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal)">17th-largest economy in the world</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">ranks 11th in GDP (nominal) per capita</a>. The Netherlands has low income inequality, but wealth inequality is relatively high.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Despite ranking 11th in <a href="/wiki/GDP_per_capita" class="mw-redirect" title="GDP per capita">GDP per capita</a>, <a href="/wiki/UNICEF" title="UNICEF">UNICEF</a> ranked the Netherlands 1st in child well-being in rich countries, both in 2007 and in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a> is the financial and business capital of the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-ez_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ez-165">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam_Stock_Exchange" class="mw-redirect" title="Amsterdam Stock Exchange">Amsterdam Stock Exchange</a> (AEX), part of <a href="/wiki/Euronext" title="Euronext">Euronext</a>, is the world's oldest stock exchange and is one of Europe's largest bourses. It is situated near <a href="/wiki/Dam_Square" title="Dam Square">Dam Square</a> in the city's centre. As a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a>, the Netherlands replaced (for accounting purposes) its former currency, the "gulden" (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_guilder" title="Dutch guilder">guilder</a>), on 1 January 1999, along with 15 other adopters of the euro. Actual <a href="/wiki/Euro_coins" title="Euro coins">euro coins</a> and <a href="/wiki/Euro_banknotes" title="Euro banknotes">banknotes</a> followed on 1 January 2002. One euro was equivalent to 2.20371 Dutch guilders. In the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">United States dollar</a> is used instead of the euro.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/220px-BlueEurozone.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/330px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/440px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="645" data-file-height="690" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Netherlands is part of a monetary union, the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a> (dark blue), and the <a href="/wiki/Internal_Market_(European_Union)" class="mw-redirect" title="Internal Market (European Union)">EU single market</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The Dutch location gives it prime access to markets in the UK and Germany, with the <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Rotterdam" title="Port of Rotterdam">Port of Rotterdam</a> being the largest port in Europe. Other important parts of the economy are <a href="/wiki/International_trade" title="International trade">international trade</a> (Dutch colonialism started with co-operative private enterprises such as the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company" title="Dutch East India Company">Dutch East India Company</a>), banking and transport. The Netherlands successfully addressed the issue of public finances and stagnating job growth long before its European partners. Amsterdam is the 5th-busiest tourist destination in Europe with more than 4.2&#160;million international visitors.<sup id="cite_ref-42milvisitors_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42milvisitors-167">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Since the enlargement of the EU large numbers of <a href="/wiki/Migrant_worker" title="Migrant worker">migrant workers</a> have arrived in the Netherlands from <a href="/wiki/Central_Europe" title="Central Europe">Central</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands continues to be one of the leading European nations for attracting foreign direct investment and is one of the five largest investors in the United States. The economy experienced a slowdown in 2005, but in 2006 recovered to the fastest pace in six years on the back of increased exports and strong investment. The pace of job growth reached 10-year highs in 2007. The Netherlands is the fourth-most competitive economy in the world, according to the <a href="/wiki/World_Economic_Forum" title="World Economic Forum">World Economic Forum</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Global_Competitiveness_Report" title="Global Competitiveness Report">Global Competitiveness Report</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wefcomp_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wefcomp-169">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Natural_gas_NL.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Natural_gas_NL.png/220px-Natural_gas_NL.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="206" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Natural_gas_NL.png/330px-Natural_gas_NL.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Natural_gas_NL.png/440px-Natural_gas_NL.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="1032" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Natural_gas_NL.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Natural gas concessions in the Netherlands. The Netherlands accounts for more than 25% of all natural gas reserves in the EU.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg/220px-Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg/330px-Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg/440px-Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4937" data-file-height="3262" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Wildervank_natural_gas_field.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Groningen_gas_field" title="Groningen gas field">Groningen gas field</a> whose discovery in 1959 transformed the Dutch economy, is one of the largest <a href="/wiki/Natural-gas_field" class="mw-redirect" title="Natural-gas field">natural-gas fields</a> in the world</div></div></div>
<p>Beginning in the 1950s, the Netherlands discovered huge natural gas resources. The sale of natural gas generated enormous revenues for the Netherlands for decades, adding, over sixty years, hundreds of billions of euros to the government's budget.<sup id="cite_ref-vorige.nrc.nl_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vorige.nrc.nl-170">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> However, the unforeseen consequences of the country's huge energy wealth impacted the competitiveness of other sectors of the economy, leading to the theory of <a href="/wiki/Dutch_disease" title="Dutch disease">Dutch disease</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-vorige.nrc.nl_170-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vorige.nrc.nl-170">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> The field is operated by government-owned Gasunie and output is jointly exploited by the government, Royal Dutch Shell, and Exxon Mobil. Gas production caused <a href="/wiki/Earthquake" title="Earthquake">earthquakes</a> which damaged housing. After a large public backlash, the government decided to phase out gas production from the field.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-171">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The Netherlands has made notable progress in its <a href="/wiki/Energy_transition" title="Energy transition">transition</a> to a <a href="/wiki/Carbon-neutral_economy" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon-neutral economy">carbon-neutral economy</a>. Thanks to increasing <a href="/wiki/Energy_conservation" title="Energy conservation">energy efficiency</a>, energy demand shows signs of decoupling from economic growth. The share of energy from <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy" title="Renewable energy">renewable sources</a> doubled from 2008 to 2019, with especially strong growth in <a href="/wiki/Offshore_wind_power" title="Offshore wind power">offshore wind</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rooftop_solar_power" title="Rooftop solar power">rooftop solar</a>. However, the Netherlands remains heavily reliant on fossil fuels and has a concentration of energy- and emission-intensive industries that will not be easy to decarbonise. Its <a href="/w/index.php?title=Klimaatakkoord&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Klimaatakkoord (page does not exist)">2019 Climate Agreement</a> defines policies and measures to support the achievement Dutch climate targets and was developed through a collaborative process involving parties from across Dutch society.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> As of 2018, the Netherlands had one of the highest rates of <a href="/wiki/Carbon_dioxide" title="Carbon dioxide">carbon dioxide</a> emissions per person in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture_and_natural_resources">Agriculture and natural resources</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_(look_the_background)_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_%28look_the_background%29_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_belted_cows_at_Park_Sonsbeek_meadows_in_the_centre_of_the_city_of_Arnhem_(look_the_background)_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cows near the city of <a href="/wiki/Arnhem" title="Arnhem">Arnhem</a></div></div></div>
<p>From a biological resource perspective, the Netherlands has a low endowment: the Netherlands’ <a href="/wiki/Biocapacity" title="Biocapacity">biocapacity</a> totals only 0.8 global hectares per person in 2016, 0.2 of which are dedicated to agriculture.<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-174">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch biocapacity per person is just about half of the 1.6 global hectares of biocapacity per person available worldwide.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> In contrast, in 2016, the Dutch used on average 4.8 global hectares of biocapacity - their <a href="/wiki/Ecological_footprint" title="Ecological footprint">ecological footprint</a> of consumption. This means the Dutch required nearly six times as much biocapacity as the Netherlands contains. As a result, the Netherlands was running a biocapacity deficit of 4.0 global hectares per person in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_174-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-174">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the Dutch waste more food than any other EU citizen, at over three times the EU average.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Dutch agricultural sector is highly mechanised, and has a strong focus on international exports. It employs about 4% of the Dutch labour force but produces large surpluses in the food-processing industry and accounts for 21% of the Dutch total export value.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch rank first in the European Union and second worldwide in value of agricultural exports, behind only the United States,<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> with agricultural exports earning €80.7&#160;billion in 2014,<sup id="cite_ref-government.nl_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.nl-179">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> up from €75.4&#160;billion in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-hollandtrade.com_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hollandtrade.com-35">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> In 2019 agricultural exports were worth €94.5 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> In an effort to reduce <a href="/wiki/Agricultural_pollution" title="Agricultural pollution">agricultural pollution</a>, the Dutch government is imposing strict limits on the productivity of the farming sector, triggering <a href="/wiki/Dutch_farmers%27_protests" title="Dutch farmers&#39; protests">Dutch farmers' protests</a>, who fear for their livelihoods.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One-third of the world's exports of <a href="/wiki/Capsicum" title="Capsicum">chilis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tomato" title="Tomato">tomatoes</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Cucumber" title="Cucumber">cucumbers</a> go through the country. The Netherlands also exports one-fifteenth of the world's apples.<sup id="cite_ref-USDA_Foreign_Agriculture_Service_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDA_Foreign_Agriculture_Service-182">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> A significant portion of Dutch agricultural exports consists of fresh-cut plants, flowers, and flower bulbs, with the Netherlands exporting two-thirds of the world's total.<sup id="cite_ref-USDA_Foreign_Agriculture_Service_182-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDA_Foreign_Agriculture_Service-182">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><br />
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_the_Netherlands" title="Demography of the Netherlands">Demography of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch people</a></div>
<p>The Netherlands had an estimated population of 17,493,969 as of 30 April 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-RegionalPop_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RegionalPop-152">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> It is the <a href="/wiki/Area_and_population_of_European_countries" title="Area and population of European countries">5th most densely populated</a> country in <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a>, and except for Malta and very small <a href="/wiki/City-states" class="mw-redirect" title="City-states">city-states</a> like <a href="/wiki/Monaco" title="Monaco">Monaco</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> and <a href="/wiki/San_Marino" title="San Marino">San Marino</a>, it is the most densely populated country in Europe. And it is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_population_density" class="mw-redirect" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by population density">16th most densely populated</a> country in the world with a density of 424 per square kilometre (1,100/sq&#160;mi). It is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by population">67th most populous country</a> in the world. Between 1900 and 1950, the country's population almost doubled from 5.1 to 10 million. From 1950 to 2000, the population further increased, to 15.9&#160;million, though this represented a lower rate of <a href="/wiki/Population_growth" title="Population growth">population growth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The estimated growth rate in 2013<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Netherlands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> is 0.44%.<sup id="cite_ref-cia-worldfactbook-nl_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-worldfactbook-nl-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlandspop.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Netherlandspop.svg/220px-Netherlandspop.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Netherlandspop.svg/330px-Netherlandspop.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Netherlandspop.svg/440px-Netherlandspop.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Netherlandspop.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Population_pyramid" title="Population pyramid">Population pyramid</a> of the Netherlands in 2017</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Fertility_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Fertility rate">fertility rate</a> in the Netherlands is 1.78 children per woman (2018 estimate),<sup id="cite_ref-cia-worldfactbook-nl_184-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-worldfactbook-nl-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> which is high compared with many other European countries, but <a href="/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility" title="Sub-replacement fertility">below the rate of 2.1 children per woman required for natural population replacement</a>, it remains considerably below the high of 5.39 children born per woman in 1879.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Netherlands subsequently has one of the oldest populations in the world, with the average age of 42.7 years.<sup id="cite_ref-cia-worldfactbook-nl_184-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-worldfactbook-nl-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">Life expectancy</a> is high in the Netherlands: 84.3 years for newborn girls and 79.7 for boys (2020 estimate).<sup id="cite_ref-cia-worldfactbook-nl_184-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-worldfactbook-nl-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch are the tallest people in the world, by nationality,<sup id="cite_ref-Science_2015-04-07_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Science_2015-04-07-186">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> with an average height of 1.81 metres (5&#160;ft 11.3&#160;in) for men and 1.67 metres (5&#160;ft 5.7&#160;in) for women in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> The average height of young men in the Netherlands increased from 5 feet, 4 inches to approximately 6 feet between the 1850s until the early 2000s.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country has a <a href="/wiki/Human_migration" title="Human migration">migration</a> rate of 1.9 migrants per 1,000 inhabitants per year.<sup id="cite_ref-cia-worldfactbook-nl_184-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-worldfactbook-nl-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> The majority of the population of the Netherlands is ethnically <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch</a>. In 2022, the population was 74.8% ethnically Dutch, 8.3% other European, 2.4% <a href="/wiki/Turkish-Dutch" class="mw-redirect" title="Turkish-Dutch">Turkish</a>, 2.4% <a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Moroccan</a>, 2.0% <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesian</a>, 2.0% <a href="/wiki/Surinamese_people" title="Surinamese people">Surinamese</a>, and 8.1% others.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Some 150,000 to 200,000 people living in the Netherlands are <a href="/wiki/Expatriate" title="Expatriate">expatriates</a>, mostly concentrated in and around <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Hague" title="The Hague">The Hague</a>, now constituting almost 10% of the population of these cities.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg/220px-Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg/330px-Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg/440px-Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4090" data-file-height="2727" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Grote_drukte_zomercarnaval_Rotterdam.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>In <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam" title="Rotterdam">Rotterdam</a> almost half the population has an immigrant background.</div></div></div>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a>, in 2010 there were 1.8 million <a href="/wiki/Foreign_born" title="Foreign born">foreign-born residents</a> in the Netherlands, corresponding to 11.1% of the total population. Of these, 1.4 million (8.5%) were born outside the EU and 0.43 million (2.6%) were born in another EU Member State.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> On 21 November 2016, there were 3.8 million residents in the Netherlands with at least one foreign-born parent ("migration background").<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Over half the young people in Amsterdam and Rotterdam have a non-western background.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> Dutch people, or <a href="/wiki/Dutch_diaspora" title="Dutch diaspora">descendants of Dutch people</a>, are also found in migrant communities worldwide, notably in <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau" title="United States Census Bureau">United States Census Bureau</a> (2006), more than 5&#160;million Americans claim total or partial <a href="/wiki/Dutch_American" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch American">Dutch</a> ancestry.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> There are close to 3&#160;million Dutch-descended <a href="/wiki/Afrikaner" class="mw-redirect" title="Afrikaner">Afrikaners</a> living in South Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Randstad" title="Randstad">Randstad</a> is the country's largest <a href="/wiki/Conurbation" title="Conurbation">conurbation</a> located in the west of the country and contains the four largest cities: <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a> in the province <a href="/wiki/North_Holland" title="North Holland">North Holland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam" title="Rotterdam">Rotterdam</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Hague" title="The Hague">The Hague</a> in the province <a href="/wiki/South_Holland" title="South Holland">South Holland</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Utrecht_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Utrecht (city)">Utrecht</a> in the province <a href="/wiki/Utrecht_(province)" title="Utrecht (province)">Utrecht</a>. The Randstad has a population of about 8.2&#160;million inhabitants<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> and is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_in_Europe_by_population" class="mw-redirect" title="List of metropolitan areas in Europe by population">5th largest</a> <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_area" title="Metropolitan area">metropolitan area</a> in Europe. According to Dutch Central Statistics Bureau, in 2015, 28 per cent of the Dutch population had a spendable income above 45,000 euros (which does not include spending on health care or education).<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
