<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_the_Netherlands" title="Languages of the Netherlands">Languages of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:352px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Foreign_languages_Netherlands,_2005.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png/350px-Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png" decoding="async" width="350" height="208" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png/525px-Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png/700px-Foreign_languages_Netherlands%2C_2005.png 2x" data-file-width="712" data-file-height="424" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Foreign_languages_Netherlands,_2005.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Knowledge of foreign languages in the Netherlands, among population 15 and older, in 2006<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The official language is <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>, which is spoken by the vast majority of the inhabitants. The dialects most spoken in the Netherlands are the <a href="/wiki/Brabantian" class="mw-redirect" title="Brabantian">Brabantian</a>-<a href="/wiki/Hollandic" class="mw-redirect" title="Hollandic">Hollandic</a> dialects.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Besides Dutch, <a href="/wiki/West_Frisian_language" title="West Frisian language">West Frisian</a> is recognised as a second official language in the northern province of <a href="/wiki/Friesland" title="Friesland">Friesland</a> (<i>Fryslân</i> in West Frisian).<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> West Frisian has a formal status for government correspondence in that province. Four other languages are protected under the <a href="/wiki/European_Charter_for_Regional_or_Minority_Languages" title="European Charter for Regional or Minority Languages">European Charter for Regional or Minority Languages</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> The first of these recognised regional languages is <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Low_Saxon" title="Dutch Low Saxon">Low Saxon</a> (<i>Nedersaksisch</i> in Dutch). Low Saxon consists of several dialects of the <a href="/wiki/Low_German" title="Low German">Low German</a> language spoken in the north and east of the Netherlands, like <a href="/wiki/Tweants" class="mw-redirect" title="Tweants">Tweants</a> in the region of <a href="/wiki/Twente" title="Twente">Twente</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Drents" class="mw-redirect" title="Drents">Drents</a> in the province of <a href="/wiki/Drenthe" title="Drenthe">Drenthe</a>.
</p><p>Secondly, <a href="/wiki/Limburgish" title="Limburgish">Limburgish</a> is also recognised as a regional language. It consists of Dutch varieties of <a href="/wiki/Meuse-Rhenish" title="Meuse-Rhenish">Meuse-Rhenish</a> <a href="/wiki/Franconian_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Franconian languages">Franconian languages</a> and is spoken in the south-eastern province of <a href="/wiki/Limburg_(Netherlands)" title="Limburg (Netherlands)">Limburg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-international2000_106-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-international2000-106">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Yiddish" title="Yiddish">Yiddish</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Romani_language" title="Romani language">Romani language</a> were recognised in 1996 as non-territorial languages.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-204">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/English_in_the_Netherlands" title="English in the Netherlands">English</a> has a formal status in the special municipalities of <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>. It is widely spoken on these islands. <a href="/wiki/Papiamento" title="Papiamento">Papiamento</a> has a formal status in the special municipality of <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_204-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-204">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The Netherlands has a long tradition of learning foreign languages, formalised in Dutch education laws. Some 90% of the total population indicate <a href="/wiki/English_in_the_Netherlands" title="English in the Netherlands">they are able to converse in English</a>, 70% in German, and 29% in French.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> English is a mandatory course in all secondary schools.<sup id="cite_ref-rijksoverheid_206-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rijksoverheid-206">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> In most lower level secondary school educations (<a href="/wiki/Voorbereidend_middelbaar_beroepsonderwijs" title="Voorbereidend middelbaar beroepsonderwijs">vmbo</a>), one additional modern foreign language is mandatory during the first two years.<sup id="cite_ref-Schedule_of_the_Central_Exams_of_2009_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schedule_of_the_Central_Exams_of_2009-207">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> In higher level secondary schools (<a href="/wiki/Hoger_algemeen_voortgezet_onderwijs" title="Hoger algemeen voortgezet onderwijs">havo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Voorbereidend_wetenschappelijk_onderwijs" title="Voorbereidend wetenschappelijk onderwijs">vwo</a>), the acquisition of two additional modern foreign language skills is mandatory during the first three years. During the last three years in vwo only one foreign language is mandatory. Besides English, the standard modern languages are <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> and <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>, although schools can replace one of these modern languages with <a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turkish_language" title="Turkish language">Turkish</a> or <a href="/wiki/Arabic" title="Arabic">Arabic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Examenblad_talen_vwo_2019_208-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Examenblad_talen_vwo_2019-208">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, schools in Friesland teach and have exams in <a href="/wiki/West_Frisian_language" title="West Frisian language">West Frisian</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> and secondary schools (called <a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">Gymnasium</a>) across the country teach <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greek" title="Ancient Greek">Ancient Greek</a> and <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_the_Netherlands" title="Religion in the Netherlands">Religion in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 15915.284869129px; border-left-color:Gold"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Gold"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Gold"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 295.09804091867px; border-left-color:Darkgreen"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Darkgreen"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Darkgreen"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 130.60549896243px; border-left-color:DarkBlue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:DarkBlue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:DarkBlue"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 1.256703212338px; border-left-color:Purple"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Purple"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Purple"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:100px; border-width:33.281954452299px 94.299053589286px 0 0; border-top-color:Gray"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Gray"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religious identification in the Netherlands (2020)<sup id="cite_ref-cbs2021_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cbs2021-8">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gray; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Irreligious" class="mw-redirect" title="Irreligious">Irreligious</a> (55.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Purple; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> (19.8%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:DarkBlue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> (14.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Darkgreen; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam_in_the_Netherlands" title="Islam in the Netherlands">Muslim</a> (5.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gold; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other (5.1%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>The population of the Netherlands was predominantly <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a> until the late 20th century, divided into a number of denominations. Although significant religious diversity remains, there has been a decline of religious adherence. The Netherlands is now one of the most <a href="/wiki/Secular" class="mw-redirect" title="Secular">secular</a> societies in the world.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 2020, <a href="/wiki/Statistics_Netherlands" title="Statistics Netherlands">Statistics Netherlands</a> found that 55% of the total population declared itself to be <a href="/wiki/Non-religious" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-religious">non-religious</a>. Groups that represent the non-religious in the Netherlands include <a href="/wiki/Humanistisch_Verbond" title="Humanistisch Verbond">Humanistisch Verbond</a>. <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholics</a> comprised 19.8% of the total population, <a href="/wiki/Protestants" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestants">Protestants</a> (14.4%). <a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslims</a> comprised 5.2% of the total population and followers of other Christian denominations and other religions (like <a href="/wiki/Judaism" title="Judaism">Judaism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a>) comprised the remaining 5.1%.<sup id="cite_ref-cbs2021_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cbs2021-8">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> A 2015 survey from another source found that Protestants outnumbered Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The southern provinces of <a href="/wiki/North_Brabant" title="North Brabant">North Brabant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Limburg_(Netherlands)" title="Limburg (Netherlands)">Limburg</a> have historically been strongly Catholic, and some residents consider the Catholic Church as a base for their <a href="/wiki/Cultural_Christian" class="mw-redirect" title="Cultural Christian">cultural identity</a>. Protestantism in the Netherlands consists of a number of churches within various traditions. The largest of these is the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Church_in_the_Netherlands" title="Protestant Church in the Netherlands">Protestant Church in the Netherlands</a> (PKN), a <a href="/wiki/United_and_uniting_churches" title="United and uniting churches">united</a> church which is <a href="/wiki/Calvinist" class="mw-redirect" title="Calvinist">Calvinist</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a> in orientation.<sup id="cite_ref-CBS_2009_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CBS_2009-211">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> It was formed in 2004 as a merger of the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Reformed_Church" title="Dutch Reformed Church">Dutch Reformed Church</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Reformed_Churches_in_the_Netherlands" title="Reformed Churches in the Netherlands">Reformed Churches in the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Lutheran_Church_in_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Evangelical Lutheran Church in the Kingdom of the Netherlands">a smaller Lutheran Church</a>. Several orthodox Calvinist and liberal churches did not merge into the PKN. Although in the Netherlands as a whole Christianity has become a minority, the Netherlands contains a <a href="/wiki/Bible_Belt_(Netherlands)" title="Bible Belt (Netherlands)">Bible Belt</a> from <a href="/wiki/Zeeland" title="Zeeland">Zeeland</a> to the northern parts of the province <a href="/wiki/Overijssel" title="Overijssel">Overijssel</a>, in which Protestant (particularly Calvinist) beliefs remain strong, and even has majorities in municipal councils<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> is the second largest religion in the state.<sup id="cite_ref-CBS2_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CBS2-212">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> The Muslim population increased from the 1960 as a result of large numbers of <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_the_Netherlands#Migration_and_ethnicity" title="Demography of the Netherlands">migrant workers</a>. This included migrant workers from <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a> and <a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Morocco</a>, as well as migrants from former <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch colonies</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Surinam_(Dutch_colony)" title="Surinam (Dutch colony)">Surinam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>. During the 1990s, <a href="/wiki/Muslim_refugees" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim refugees">Muslim refugees</a> arrived from countries like <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Iran</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iraq" title="Iraq">Iraq</a>, <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another religion practised is <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a>, with around 215,000 adherents (slightly over 1% of the population). Most of these are <a href="/wiki/Indo-Surinamese" title="Indo-Surinamese">Indo-Surinamese</a>. There are also sizeable populations of Hindu immigrants from <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a>, and some Western adherents of <a href="/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_West" title="Hinduism in the West">Hinduism-orientated new religious movements</a> such as <a href="/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness" title="International Society for Krishna Consciousness">Hare Krishnas</a>. The Netherlands has an estimated 250,000 <a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_the_Netherlands" title="Buddhism in the Netherlands">Buddhists</a> or people strongly attracted to this religion, mainly ethnic Dutch people. In addition, there are about 45,000 <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_the_Netherlands" title="History of the Jews in the Netherlands">Jews</a> in the Netherlands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Constitution of the Netherlands guarantees freedom of education, which means that all schools that adhere to general quality criteria receive the same government funding. This includes schools based on religious principles by religious groups (especially Catholic and various Protestant). Three political parties in the Dutch parliament, (<a href="/wiki/Christian_Democratic_Appeal" title="Christian Democratic Appeal">CDA</a>, and two small parties, <a href="/wiki/ChristianUnion" class="mw-redirect" title="ChristianUnion">ChristianUnion</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reformed_Political_Party" title="Reformed Political Party">SGP</a>) are based upon the Christian belief. Several Christian religious holidays are national holidays (Christmas, Easter, <a href="/wiki/Pentecost" title="Pentecost">Pentecost</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Ascension_of_Jesus" title="Ascension of Jesus">Ascension of Jesus</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Upon the country's independence, Protestants were predominant in most of the country, while Roman Catholics were dominant in the south, especially North Brabant and Limburg. In the late 19th century, secularism, atheism and <a href="/wiki/Pillarisation" title="Pillarisation">pillarisation</a> gained adherents. By 1960, Catholics equalled Protestants in number; thereafter, both Christian branches began to decline. Conversely, Islam grew considerably as the result of <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_the_Netherlands#Migration_and_ethnicity" title="Demography of the Netherlands">immigration</a>. Since 2000 there has been raised awareness of religion, mainly due to <a href="/wiki/Muslim_extremism" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim extremism">Muslim extremism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A survey in December 2014 concluded that for the first time there were more atheists (25%) than theists (17%) in the Netherlands, while the remainder of the population was agnostic (31%) or <a href="/wiki/Ietsism" title="Ietsism">ietsistic</a> (27%).<sup id="cite_ref-trouw.nl_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-trouw.nl-216">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, a vast majority of the inhabitants of the Netherlands (82%) said they had never or almost never visited a church, and 59% stated that they had never been to a church of any kind. Of all the people questioned, 24% saw themselves as atheist, an increase of 11% compared to the previous study done in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-BerntsA_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BerntsA-217">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> The expected rise of spirituality (ietsism) has come to a halt according to research in 2015. In 2006, 40% of respondents considered themselves spiritual; in 2015 this has dropped to 31%. The number who believed in the existence of a higher power fell from 36% to 28% over the same period.<sup id="cite_ref-nos.nl_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nos.nl-218">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_the_Netherlands" title="Education in the Netherlands">Education in the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Universities_in_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Universities in the Netherlands">Universities in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg/220px-BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg/330px-BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg/440px-BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3500" data-file-height="2326" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BSN_Junior_School_Vlaskamp.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An international primary school in The Hague</div></div></div>
<p>Education in the Netherlands is compulsory between the ages of 5 and 16. If a child does not have a "starting qualification" (HAVO, VWO or MBO 2+ degree) they are still forced to attend classes until they achieve such a qualification or reach the age of 18.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Children in the Netherlands attend elementary school from (on average) ages 4 to 12. It has eight grades and first is facultative. Based on an aptitude test, the eighth grade teacher's recommendation and the opinion of the pupil's parents or caretakers, a choice is made for one of the three main streams of secondary education. After completing a particular stream, a pupil may still continue in the penultimate year of the next stream.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Utrecht-Uithof,_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg/220px-Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg/330px-Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg/440px-Utrecht-Uithof%2C_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Utrecht-Uithof,_from_CambridgeLaan_01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View on the Utrecht Science Park of <a href="/wiki/Utrecht_University" title="Utrecht University">Utrecht University</a>. The building in the centre is the library.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Voorbereidend_middelbaar_beroepsonderwijs" title="Voorbereidend middelbaar beroepsonderwijs">VMBO</a> has four grades and is subdivided over several levels. Successfully completing the VMBO results in a low-level vocational degree that grants access to the MBO. The MBO (middle-level applied education) is a form of education that primarily focuses on teaching a practical trade or a vocational degree. With the MBO certification, a student can apply for the HBO. The <a href="/wiki/Hoger_algemeen_voortgezet_onderwijs" title="Hoger algemeen voortgezet onderwijs">HAVO</a> has 5 grades and allows for admission to the HBO. The HBO (higher professional education) are <a href="/wiki/Vocational_university" title="Vocational university">universities of professional education</a> (applied sciences) that award professional bachelor's degrees; similar to polytechnic degrees. An HBO degree gives access to the university system. The <a href="/wiki/Voorbereidend_wetenschappelijk_onderwijs" title="Voorbereidend wetenschappelijk onderwijs">VWO</a> (comprising <a href="/wiki/Atheneum_(school)" title="Atheneum (school)">atheneum</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">gymnasium</a>) has 6 grades and prepares for studying at a research university. Universities offer a three-year bachelor's degree, followed by a one or two-year master's degree, which in turn can be followed by a four or five-year <a href="/wiki/Doctoral_degree" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctoral degree">doctoral degree</a> programme. 
</p><p>Doctoral candidates in the Netherlands are generally non-tenured employees of a university. All Dutch schools and universities are publicly funded and managed with the exception of religious schools that are publicly funded but not managed by the state even though requirements are necessary for the funding to be authorised. Dutch universities have a tuition fee of about 2,000 euros a year for students from the Netherlands and the European Union. The amount is about 10,000 euros for non-EU students.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Healthcare">Healthcare</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_the_Netherlands" title="Healthcare in the Netherlands">Healthcare in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_(1632-1723)._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg/220px-Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="261" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg/330px-Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg/440px-Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_%281632-1723%29._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="2148" data-file-height="2547" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthonie_van_Leeuwenhoek_(1632-1723)._Natuurkundige_te_Delft_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-957.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Portrait of <a href="/wiki/Antonie_van_Leeuwenhoek" title="Antonie van Leeuwenhoek">Antonie van Leeuwenhoek</a> (1632–1723), known as "the father of microbiology"</div></div></div> 	
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG/220px-Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG/330px-Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG/440px-Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Meander_MC_noordzijde_1.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A public hospital in <a href="/wiki/Amersfoort" title="Amersfoort">Amersfoort</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 2016, the Netherlands maintained its number one position at the top of the annual <a href="/wiki/Euro_health_consumer_index" class="mw-redirect" title="Euro health consumer index">Euro health consumer index</a> (EHCI), which compares healthcare systems in Europe, scoring 916 of a maximum 1,000 points. The Netherlands has been among the top three countries in each report published since 2005. On 48 indicators such as patient rights and information, accessibility, prevention and outcomes, the Netherlands secured its top position among 37 European countries for six years in a row.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
The Netherlands was ranked first in a study in 2009 comparing the <a href="/wiki/Health_care_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Health care system">health care systems</a> of the United States, Australia, Canada, Germany and New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ever since a major reform of the health care system in 2006, the Dutch system received more points in the Index each year. According to the HCP (<a href="/wiki/Health_Consumer_Powerhouse" title="Health Consumer Powerhouse">Health Consumer Powerhouse</a>), the Netherlands has 'a chaos system', meaning patients have a great degree of freedom from where to buy their health insurance, to where they get their healthcare service. The difference between the Netherlands and other countries is that the chaos is managed. Healthcare decisions are being made in a dialogue between the patients and healthcare professionals.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Health insurance in the Netherlands is mandatory. Healthcare in the Netherlands is covered by two statutory forms of insurance:
</p>
<ul><li>Zorgverzekeringswet (ZVW), often called "basic insurance", covers common medical care.</li>
<li>Algemene Wet Bijzondere Ziektekosten (AWBZ) covers long-term nursing and care.</li></ul>
<p>While Dutch residents are automatically insured by the government for AWBZ, everyone has to take out their own basic healthcare insurance (basisverzekering), except those under 18 who are automatically covered under their parents' premium. If a person decides not to carry out an insurance coverage, the person may be fined. Insurers have to offer a universal package for everyone over the age of 18 years, regardless of age or state of health – it's illegal to refuse an application or impose special conditions. In contrast to many other European systems, the Dutch government is responsible for the accessibility and quality of the healthcare system in the Netherlands, but not in charge of its management.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Healthcare in the Netherlands can be divided in several ways: three echelons, in somatic and mental health care and in 'cure' (short term) and 'care' (long term). Home doctors (<i>huisartsen</i>, comparable to <a href="/wiki/General_practitioner" title="General practitioner">general practitioners</a>) form the largest part of the first echelon. Being referenced by a member of the first echelon is mandatory for access to the second and third echelon.<sup id="cite_ref-Boot_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boot-224">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> The health care system is in comparison to other Western countries quite effective but not the most cost-effective.<sup id="cite_ref-BCG_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BCG-225">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Healthcare in the Netherlands is financed by a dual system that came into effect in January 2006. Long-term treatments, especially those that involve semi-permanent hospitalisation, and also disability costs such as wheelchairs, are covered by a state-controlled mandatory insurance. This is laid down in the <i><a href="/wiki/AWBZ" title="AWBZ">Algemene Wet Bijzondere Ziektekosten</a></i> ("General Law on Exceptional Healthcare Costs") which first came into effect in 1968. In 2009 this insurance covered 27% of all health care expenses.<sup id="cite_ref-statline.cbs.nl_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statline.cbs.nl-226">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For all regular (short-term) medical treatment, there is a system of obligatory <a href="/wiki/Health_insurance" title="Health insurance">health insurance</a>, with private health insurance companies. These insurance companies are obliged to provide a package with a defined set of insured treatments.<sup id="cite_ref-minvws.nl_227-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-minvws.nl-227">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> This insurance covers 41% of all health care expenses.<sup id="cite_ref-statline.cbs.nl_226-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statline.cbs.nl-226">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other sources of health care payment are taxes (14%), out of pocket payments (9%), additional optional health insurance packages (4%) and a range of other sources (4%).<sup id="cite_ref-statline.cbs.nl_226-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statline.cbs.nl-226">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Affordability is guaranteed through a system of income-related allowances and individual and employer-paid income-related premiums.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>A key feature of the Dutch system is that premiums may not be related to health status or age. Risk variances between private health insurance companies due to the different risks presented by individual policy holders are compensated through <a href="/wiki/Risk_equalization" title="Risk equalization">risk equalisation</a> and a common <a href="/wiki/Risk_pool" title="Risk pool">risk pool</a>. The funding burden for all short-term health care coverage is carried 50% by employers, 45% by the insured person and 5% by the government. Children under 18 are covered for free. Those on low incomes receive compensation to help them pay their insurance. Premiums paid by the insured are about €100 per month (about US$127 in August 2010 and €150 or US$196 in 2012), with variation of about 5% between the various competing insurers, and a yearly deductible of €220 (US$288).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_the_Netherlands" title="Transport in the Netherlands">Transport in the Netherlands</a></div>
<p>Mobility on Dutch roads has grown continuously since the 1950s and now exceeds 200 billion km travelled per year,<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> three quarters of which are done by car.<sup id="cite_ref-OECD-ITF_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OECD-ITF-229">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Around half of all trips in the Netherlands are made by car, 25% by bicycle, 20% walking, and 5% by public transport.<sup id="cite_ref-OECD-ITF_229-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OECD-ITF-229">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Road_transport">Road transport</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg/220px-A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg/330px-A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg/440px-A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:A1-Hoog_Buurlo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/A1_motorway_(Netherlands)" title="A1 motorway (Netherlands)">A1 motorway</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Gelderland" title="Gelderland">Gelderland</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands has one of the densest road networks in the world—much denser than Germany and France, but still not as dense as Belgium.<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> The Netherlands has a relatively high uptake of <a href="/wiki/Electric_vehicle" title="Electric vehicle">electric vehicles</a>, as the government implemented ambitious policy on both charging infrastructure and tax benefits.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, the Netherlands hosts approximately 30% of all recharging stations in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> Moreover, newly sold cars in the Netherlands have on average the lowest CO<sub style="font-size: 80%;vertical-align: -0.35em">2</sub> emissions in the EU.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_transport">Public transport</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_(SLT)_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_(22087244596).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg/220px-NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg/330px-NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg/440px-NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_%28SLT%29_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_%2822087244596%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:NSR_-_Sprinter_Lighttrain_(SLT)_-_Wieldrechtse_Zeedijk_-_Dordrecht_(22087244596).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A regional train operated by <a href="/wiki/Nederlandse_Spoorwegen" title="Nederlandse Spoorwegen">Nederlandse Spoorwegen</a> (NS)</div></div></div>
<p>About 13% of all distance is travelled by public transport, the majority of which by train.<sup id="cite_ref-OECD-ITF_229-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OECD-ITF-229">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Like in <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size" title="List of countries by rail transport network size">many other European countries</a>, the Dutch rail network of 3,013&#160;km route is also rather dense.<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> The network is mostly focused on passenger rail services and connects all major towns and cities, with over <a href="/wiki/Railway_stations_in_the_Netherlands" title="Railway stations in the Netherlands">400 stations</a>. Trains are frequent, with two trains per hour on lesser lines,<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;i&#93;</a></sup> two to four trains per hour on average, and up to eight trains an hour on the busiest lines.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch national train network also includes the <a href="/wiki/HSL-Zuid" title="HSL-Zuid">HSL-Zuid</a>, a high-speed line between the <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam_metropolitan_area" title="Amsterdam metropolitan area">Amsterdam metropolitan area</a> and the Belgian border for trains running from Paris and London to the Netherlands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cycling">Cycling</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cycling_in_the_Netherlands" title="Cycling in the Netherlands">Cycling in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg/220px-Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg/330px-Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg/440px-Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4555" data-file-height="3064" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bike_entrance_Rotterdam_Central_Station.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Bike passage at <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam_Centraal_station" title="Rotterdam Centraal station">Rotterdam Centraal station</a></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Cycling_in_the_Netherlands" title="Cycling in the Netherlands">Cycling</a> is a ubiquitous mode of transport in the Netherlands. Almost as many kilometres are covered by bicycle as by train.<sup id="cite_ref-OECD-ITF_229-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OECD-ITF-229">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch are estimated to have at least 18 million bicycles,<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> which makes more than one per capita, and twice as many as the circa 9 million motor vehicles on the road.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, the <a href="/wiki/European_Cyclists%27_Federation" title="European Cyclists&#39; Federation">European Cyclists' Federation</a> ranked both the Netherlands and <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a> as the most bike-friendly countries in Europe,<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> but more of the Dutch (36%) than of the Danes (23%) list the bike as their most frequent mode of transport on a typical day.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;j&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cycling_infrastructure" title="Cycling infrastructure">Cycling infrastructure</a> is comprehensive. Busy roads have received some 35,000&#160;km of <a href="/wiki/Segregated_cycle_facilities" class="mw-redirect" title="Segregated cycle facilities">dedicated cycle tracks</a>, physically segregated from motorised traffic.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Busy junctions are often equipped with bicycle-specific traffic lights. There are large bicycle parking facilities, particularly in city centres and at train stations.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Water_transport">Water transport</span></h3>
<p>Until the introduction of trains, ships were the primary mode of transport in the Netherlands. And shipping has remained crucial afterwards. The <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Rotterdam" title="Port of Rotterdam">Port of Rotterdam</a> is the largest port in Europe and the largest port in the world outside East-Asia, with the rivers Meuse and Rhine providing excellent access to the <a href="/wiki/Hinterland" title="Hinterland">hinterland</a> upstream reaching to <a href="/wiki/Basel" title="Basel">Basel</a>, Switzerland, and into Germany and France. As of 2022<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Netherlands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Rotterdam was the world's tenth largest <a href="/wiki/Container_port" title="Container port">container port</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> The port's main activities are <a href="/wiki/Petrochemical" title="Petrochemical">petrochemical</a> industries and general cargo handling and <a href="/wiki/Transshipment" title="Transshipment">transshipment</a>. The harbour functions as an important transit point for <a href="/wiki/Bulk_material_handling" title="Bulk material handling">bulk materials</a> and between the European continent and overseas. From Rotterdam goods are transported by ship, river barge, train or road. The Volkeraksluizen between Rotterdam and Antwerp are the biggest sluices for inland navigation in the world in terms of tonnage passing through them. In 2007, the <a href="/wiki/Betuweroute" title="Betuweroute">Betuweroute</a>, a new fast freight railway from Rotterdam to Germany, was completed. The Netherlands also hosts Europe's 4th largest port in <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Amsterdam" title="Port of Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Inland_navigation" title="Inland navigation">inland shipping</a> fleet of the Netherlands is the largest in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> The Netherlands also has the largest fleet of active historical ships in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> Boats are used for passenger travel as well, such as the Watertaxies in Rotterdam. The ferry network in Amsterdam and the Waterbus network in Rotterdam are part of the public transport system.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Air_transport">Air transport</span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Amsterdam_Airport_Schiphol" title="Amsterdam Airport Schiphol">Schiphol Airport</a>, just southwest of Amsterdam, is the main <a href="/wiki/International_airport" title="International airport">international airport</a> in the Netherlands, and the <a href="/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Europe" title="List of the busiest airports in Europe">third busiest airport in Europe</a> by number of passengers. Schiphol is the main hub for <a href="/wiki/KLM" title="KLM">KLM</a>, the nation's <a href="/wiki/Flag_carrier" title="Flag carrier">flag carrier</a> and the world's oldest airline.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, the <a href="/wiki/Schiphol_Group" title="Schiphol Group">Royal Schiphol Group</a> airports handled 70 million passengers.<sup id="cite_ref-NOS_Schiphol64_250-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NOS_Schiphol64-250">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> All air traffic is international and Schiphol Airport is connected to over 300 destinations worldwide, more than any other European airport.<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> The airport is a major freight hub as well, processing 1.44 million tonnes of cargo in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Smaller international airports are located in or near <a href="/wiki/Eindhoven_Airport" title="Eindhoven Airport">Eindhoven</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rotterdam_The_Hague_Airport" title="Rotterdam The Hague Airport">Rotterdam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maastricht_Aachen_Airport" title="Maastricht Aachen Airport">Maastricht</a> and <a href="/wiki/Groningen_Airport_Eelde" title="Groningen Airport Eelde">Groningen</a>. Air transport is of vital significance for the Caribbean part of the Netherlands, with all islands having their own airport. This includes the shortest runway in the world on <a href="/wiki/Juancho_E._Yrausquin_Airport" title="Juancho E. Yrausquin Airport">Saba</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-253" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-253">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_the_Netherlands" title="Culture of the Netherlands">Culture of the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="center"><div class="thumb tnone"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:552px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hollande04.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Hollande04.jpg/550px-Hollande04.jpg" decoding="async" width="550" height="193" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/05/Hollande04.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="792" data-file-height="278" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hollande04.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><div style="text-align:center;">Some symbols and icons of Dutch art</div></div></div></div></div>
<h3><span id="Art.2C_architecture_and_philosophy"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Art,_architecture_and_philosophy">Art, architecture and philosophy</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Dutch_art" title="Dutch art">Dutch art</a>, <a href="/wiki/Architecture_of_the_Netherlands" title="Architecture of the Netherlands">Architecture of the Netherlands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_literature" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch literature">Dutch literature</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:292px;max-width:292px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:138px;max-width:138px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:109px;overflow:hidden"><a height="110" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Van_Gogh_-_Wasserm%C3%BChle_in_Kollen_bei_Nuenen.jpeg/222px-Van_Gogh_-_Wasserm%C3%BChle_in_Kollen_bei_Nuenen.jpeg 1.5x, Nuenen</a></i> by Brabantine artist <a href="/wiki/Vincent_van_Gogh" title="Vincent van Gogh">Vincent van Gogh</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:290px;max-width:290px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:161px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Rijksmuseum_2022.jpg" class="image"><img alt=""
