//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Rijksmuseum_2022.jpg/576px-Rijksmuseum_2022.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4582" data-file-height="2577" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">The <a href="/wiki/Rijksmuseum" title="Rijksmuseum">Rijksmuseum</a> in Amsterdam</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands has had many well-known painters. In the Middle Ages <a href="/wiki/Hieronymus_Bosch" title="Hieronymus Bosch">Hieronymus Bosch</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pieter_Bruegel_the_Elder" title="Pieter Bruegel the Elder">Pieter Bruegel the Elder</a> were leading Dutch pioneers. During the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Golden_Age" title="Dutch Golden Age">Dutch Golden Age</a>, the Dutch Republic was prosperous and witnessed a flourishing artistic movement. The "Dutch Masters", spanning this 17th century era, included <a href="/wiki/Rembrandt_Harmenszoon_van_Rijn" class="mw-redirect" title="Rembrandt Harmenszoon van Rijn">Rembrandt van Rijn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Johannes_Vermeer" title="Johannes Vermeer">Johannes Vermeer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Steen" title="Jan Steen">Jan Steen</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jacob_van_Ruisdael" title="Jacob van Ruisdael">Jacob van Ruisdael</a>. Famous Dutch painters of the 19th and 20th century included <a href="/wiki/Vincent_van_Gogh" title="Vincent van Gogh">Vincent van Gogh</a> and <a href="/wiki/Piet_Mondrian" title="Piet Mondrian">Piet Mondrian</a>. <a href="/wiki/M._C._Escher" title="M. C. Escher">M. C. Escher</a> is a well-known graphic artist.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Literature flourished as well during the Dutch Golden Age, with <a href="/wiki/Joost_van_den_Vondel" title="Joost van den Vondel">Joost van den Vondel</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pieter_Corneliszoon_Hooft" title="Pieter Corneliszoon Hooft">P. C. Hooft</a> as the most famous writers. In the 19th century, <a href="/wiki/Multatuli" title="Multatuli">Multatuli</a> wrote about the poor treatment of the natives in the Dutch colony. <a href="/wiki/The_Diary_of_a_Young_Girl" title="The Diary of a Young Girl"><i>Diary of a Young Girl</i></a> by <a href="/wiki/Anne_Frank" title="Anne Frank">Anne Frank</a> is the most translated book from Dutch. Other important 20th century authors include <a href="/wiki/Harry_Mulisch" title="Harry Mulisch">Harry Mulisch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Wolkers" title="Jan Wolkers">Jan Wolkers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hella_S._Haasse" class="mw-redirect" title="Hella S. Haasse">Hella S. Haasse</a>, <a href="/wiki/Willem_Frederik_Hermans" title="Willem Frederik Hermans">Willem Frederik Hermans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cees_Nooteboom" title="Cees Nooteboom">Cees Nooteboom</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gerard_Reve" title="Gerard Reve">Gerard Reve</a>. <a href="/wiki/Janwillem_van_de_Wetering" title="Janwillem van de Wetering">Janwillem van de Wetering</a> wrote successful detectives,  <a href="/wiki/Dick_Bruna" title="Dick Bruna">Dick Bruna</a> (doubling as illustrator) and <a href="/wiki/Annie_M._G._Schmidt" title="Annie M. G. Schmidt">Annie M. G. Schmidt</a> children's books.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Various architectural styles can be distinguished in the Netherlands. The <a href="/wiki/Romanesque_architecture" title="Romanesque architecture">Romanesque architecture</a> was built between the years 950 and 1250. This architectural style is most concentrated in the provinces of <a href="/wiki/Gelderland" title="Gelderland">Gelderland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Limburg_(Netherlands)" title="Limburg (Netherlands)">Limburg</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Gothic_architecture" title="Gothic architecture">Gothic architecture</a> was used in the Netherlands from about 1230. Gothic buildings had large windows, pointed arches and were richly decorated. <a href="/wiki/Brabantine_Gothic" title="Brabantine Gothic">Brabantine Gothic</a> originated with the rise of the Duchy of Brabant and spread throughout the Burgundian provinces. <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Baroque_architecture" title="Dutch Baroque architecture">Dutch Baroque architecture</a> (1525 – 1630) and classicism (1630 – 1700) is especially evident in the west of the Netherlands. Other common architectural styles are <a href="/wiki/Style_Louis_XIV" class="mw-redirect" title="Style Louis XIV">Style Louis XIV</a>, <a href="/wiki/Art_Nouveau" title="Art Nouveau">Art Nouveau</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rationalism_(architecture)" title="Rationalism (architecture)">Rationalism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Neoclassicism" title="Neoclassicism">Neoclassicism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Expressionist_architecture" title="Expressionist architecture">Expressionism</a>, <a href="/wiki/De_Stijl" title="De Stijl">De Stijl</a>, <a href="/wiki/Traditionalist_School_(architecture)" title="Traditionalist School (architecture)">Traditionalism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brutalism" class="mw-redirect" title="Brutalism">Brutalism</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Erasmus" title="Erasmus">Erasmus</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baruch_Spinoza" title="Baruch Spinoza">Spinoza</a> were famous Dutch philosophers. The Dutch scientist <a href="/wiki/Christiaan_Huygens" title="Christiaan Huygens">Christiaan Huygens</a> (1629–1695) discovered <a href="/wiki/Saturn" title="Saturn">Saturn</a>'s moon <a href="/wiki/Titan_(moon)" title="Titan (moon)">Titan</a>, argued that light travelled as waves, invented the <a href="/wiki/Pendulum_clock" title="Pendulum clock">pendulum clock</a>, and was the first physicist to use mathematical formulae. <a href="/wiki/Antonie_van_Leeuwenhoek" title="Antonie van Leeuwenhoek">Antonie van Leeuwenhoek</a> was the first to observe and describe <a href="/wiki/Microorganism" title="Microorganism">single-celled organisms</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Microscope" title="Microscope">microscope</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Windmill" title="Windmill">Windmills</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tulip" title="Tulip">tulips</a>, <a href="/wiki/Clog_(shoe)" class="mw-redirect" title="Clog (shoe)">wooden shoes</a>, cheese, <a href="/wiki/Delftware" title="Delftware">Delftware</a> pottery, and <a href="/wiki/Cannabis_(drug)" title="Cannabis (drug)">cannabis</a> have grown to symbolize the Netherlands, especially among tourists.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Dutch_value_system">Dutch value system</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Dutch_customs_and_etiquette" title="Dutch customs and etiquette">Dutch customs and etiquette</a></div>
<p>Dutch society is <a href="/wiki/Egalitarianism" title="Egalitarianism">egalitarian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Modernism" title="Modernism">modern</a>. The Dutch have an aversion to the non-essential.<sup id="cite_ref-Colin_White_1995_254-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colin_White_1995-254">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> Ostentatious behaviour is to be avoided. The Dutch are proud of their <a href="/wiki/Cultural_heritage" title="Cultural heritage">cultural heritage</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_art" title="Dutch art">rich history in art</a> and involvement in <a href="/wiki/International_relations" title="International relations">international affairs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Colin_White_1995_254-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colin_White_1995-254">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A Dutch saying indicating their sense of national pride in their reclamation of land from the sea and marshes is "God created the world, but the Dutch created the Netherlands."<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg/220px-Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg/330px-Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg/440px-Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3104" data-file-height="2132" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Koningsdag_2017_Amstel.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Dutch people in orange celebrating <a href="/wiki/Koningsdag" title="Koningsdag">King's Day</a> in Amsterdam, 2017</div></div></div>
<p>Dutch manners are open and direct with a no-nonsense attitude—informality combined with adherence to basic behaviour. According to a humorous source on Dutch culture, "Their directness gives many the impression that they are rude and crude—attributes they prefer to call openness."<sup id="cite_ref-Colin_White_1995_254-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colin_White_1995-254">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> A well known more serious source on Dutch etiquette is "Dealing with the Dutch" by Jacob Vossestein: "Dutch egalitarianism is the idea that people are equal, especially from a moral point of view, and accordingly, causes the somewhat ambiguous stance the Dutch have towards hierarchy and status."<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> As always, manners differ between groups. Asking about basic rules will not be considered impolite. "What may strike you as being blatantly blunt topics and comments are no more embarrassing or unusual to the Dutch than discussing the weather."<sup id="cite_ref-Colin_White_1995_254-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colin_White_1995-254">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands is one of the most secular countries of Europe, and religion in the Netherlands is generally considered as a personal matter which is not supposed to be propagated in public, although it often remains a discussion subject. For only 17% of the population religion is important and 14% goes to church weekly.<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands has a long history of social tolerance and today is regarded as a liberal country, considering <a href="/wiki/Drug_policy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Drug policy of the Netherlands">its drug policy</a> and its legalisation of <a href="/wiki/Euthanasia_in_the_Netherlands" title="Euthanasia in the Netherlands">euthanasia</a>. On 1 April 2001, the Netherlands became the first nation to legalise <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_Netherlands" title="Same-sex marriage in the Netherlands">same-sex marriage</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_the_Netherlands" title="Music of the Netherlands">Music of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Music_of_the_former_Netherlands_Antilles" title="Music of the former Netherlands Antilles">Music of the former Netherlands Antilles</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:192px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Anouk,_ESC2013_press_conference_09_(crop).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg/190px-Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="190" height="190" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg/285px-Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg/380px-Anouk%2C_ESC2013_press_conference_09_%28crop%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1864" data-file-height="1864" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Anouk,_ESC2013_press_conference_09_(crop).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pop singer <a href="/wiki/Anouk_(singer)" title="Anouk (singer)">Anouk</a> in 2013</div></div></div>
<p>The Netherlands has multiple <a href="/wiki/Music" title="Music">music</a> traditions. Traditional Dutch music is a genre known as "<a href="/wiki/Levenslied" title="Levenslied">Levenslied</a>", meaning <i>Song of life</i>, to an extent comparable to a French <a href="/wiki/Chanson" title="Chanson">Chanson</a> or a German <a href="/wiki/Schlager_music" title="Schlager music">Schlager</a>. These songs typically have a simple melody and rhythm, and a straightforward structure of verses and choruses. Themes can be light, but are often sentimental and include <a href="/wiki/Love" title="Love">love</a>, <a href="/wiki/Death" title="Death">death</a> and <a href="/wiki/Loneliness" title="Loneliness">loneliness</a>. Traditional musical instruments such as the <a href="/wiki/Accordion" title="Accordion">accordion</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Barrel_organ" title="Barrel organ">barrel organ</a> are a staple of levenslied music, though in recent years many artists also use synthesisers and guitars. Artists in this genre include <a href="/wiki/Jan_Smit_(singer)" title="Jan Smit (singer)">Jan Smit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Frans_Bauer" title="Frans Bauer">Frans Bauer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Hazes" title="André Hazes">André Hazes</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Contemporary Dutch <a href="/wiki/Rock_music" title="Rock music">rock</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pop_music" title="Pop music">pop music</a> (<a href="/wiki/Nederpop" title="Nederpop">Nederpop</a>) originated in the 1960s, heavily influenced by popular music from the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> and <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">Britain</a>. In the 1960s and 1970s the lyrics were mostly in English, and some tracks were instrumental. Bands such as <a href="/wiki/Shocking_Blue" title="Shocking Blue">Shocking Blue</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golden_Earring" title="Golden Earring">Golden Earring</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tee_Set" class="mw-redirect" title="Tee Set">Tee Set</a>, <a href="/wiki/George_Baker_Selection" title="George Baker Selection">George Baker Selection</a> and <a href="/wiki/Focus_(band)" title="Focus (band)">Focus</a> enjoyed international success. From the 1980s, more and more pop musicians started working in the Dutch language, partly inspired by the huge success of the band <a href="/wiki/Doe_Maar" title="Doe Maar">Doe Maar</a>. Today Dutch rock and pop music thrives in both languages, with some artists recording in both.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22,_Amsterdam_Arena,_June_2016_(5).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg/220px-Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg/330px-Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg/440px-Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22%2C_Amsterdam_Arena%2C_June_2016_%285%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2500" data-file-height="1667" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Coldplay_perform_%22Up%26Up%22,_Amsterdam_Arena,_June_2016_(5).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Johan_Cruyff_Arena" title="Johan Cruyff Arena">Johan Cruyff Arena</a>, the largest Dutch concert venue</div></div></div>
<p>Current <a href="/wiki/Symphonic_metal" title="Symphonic metal">symphonic metal</a> bands <a href="/wiki/Epica_(band)" title="Epica (band)">Epica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Delain" title="Delain">Delain</a>, <a href="/wiki/ReVamp" title="ReVamp">ReVamp</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Gathering_(band)" title="The Gathering (band)">The Gathering</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asrai" title="Asrai">Asrai</a>, <a href="/wiki/Autumn" title="Autumn">Autumn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ayreon" title="Ayreon">Ayreon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Within_Temptation" title="Within Temptation">Within Temptation</a> as well as <a href="/wiki/Jazz" title="Jazz">jazz</a> and pop singer <a href="/wiki/Caro_Emerald" title="Caro Emerald">Caro Emerald</a> are having international success. Also, metal bands like <a href="/wiki/Hail_of_Bullets" title="Hail of Bullets">Hail of Bullets</a>, <a href="/wiki/God_Dethroned" title="God Dethroned">God Dethroned</a>, <a href="/wiki/Izegrim" title="Izegrim">Izegrim</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asphyx" title="Asphyx">Asphyx</a>, <a href="/wiki/Textures_(band)" title="Textures (band)">Textures</a>, <a href="/wiki/Present_Danger" class="mw-redirect" title="Present Danger">Present Danger</a>, <a href="/wiki/Heidevolk" title="Heidevolk">Heidevolk</a> and <a href="/wiki/Slechtvalk" title="Slechtvalk">Slechtvalk</a> are popular guests at the biggest <a href="/wiki/Metal_festival" class="mw-redirect" title="Metal festival">metal festivals</a> in Europe. Contemporary local stars include pop singer <a href="/wiki/Anouk_(singer)" title="Anouk (singer)">Anouk</a>, country pop singer <a href="/wiki/Ilse_DeLange" title="Ilse DeLange">Ilse DeLange</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Guelderish" title="South Guelderish">South Guelderish</a> and <a href="/wiki/Limburgish" title="Limburgish">Limburgish</a> dialect singing folk band <a href="/wiki/Rowwen_H%C3%A8ze" title="Rowwen Hèze">Rowwen Hèze</a>, rock band <a href="/wiki/BL%C3%98F" title="BLØF">BLØF</a> and duo <a href="/wiki/Nick_%26_Simon" title="Nick &amp; Simon">Nick &amp; Simon</a>. <a href="/wiki/Trijntje_Oosterhuis" title="Trijntje Oosterhuis">Trijntje Oosterhuis</a>, one of the country's most well known and versatile singers, has made multiple albums with famous American composers <a href="/wiki/Vince_Mendoza" title="Vince Mendoza">Vince Mendoza</a> and <a href="/wiki/Burt_Bacharach" title="Burt Bacharach">Burt Bacharach</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Early 1990s Dutch and <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgian</a> <a href="/wiki/House_music" title="House music">house music</a> came together in <a href="/wiki/Eurodance" title="Eurodance">Eurodance</a> project <a href="/wiki/2_Unlimited" title="2 Unlimited">2 Unlimited</a>. Selling 18 million records,<sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> the two singers in the band are the most successful Dutch music artists to this day. Tracks like "<a href="/wiki/Get_Ready_for_This" title="Get Ready for This">Get Ready for This</a>" are still popular themes of U.S. sports events, like the <a href="/wiki/NHL" class="mw-redirect" title="NHL">NHL</a>. In the mid-1990s Dutch language <a href="/wiki/Hip_hop_music" title="Hip hop music">rap and hip hop</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Dutch_hip_hop" title="Dutch hip hop">Nederhop</a></i>) also came to fruition and has become popular in the Netherlands and Belgium. Artists with North African, Caribbean or Middle Eastern origins have strongly influenced this genre.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Since the 1990s, Dutch <a href="/wiki/Electronic_dance_music" title="Electronic dance music">electronic dance music</a> (EDM) gained widespread popularity in the world in many forms, from <a href="/wiki/Trance_music" title="Trance music">trance</a>, <a href="/wiki/Techno" title="Techno">techno</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gabber" title="Gabber">gabber</a> to <a href="/wiki/Hardstyle" title="Hardstyle">hardstyle</a>. Some of the world's best known dance music DJs hail from the Netherlands, including <a href="/wiki/Armin_van_Buuren" title="Armin van Buuren">Armin van Buuren</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ti%C3%ABsto" title="Tiësto">Tiësto</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hardwell" title="Hardwell">Hardwell</a>, <a href="/wiki/Martin_Garrix" title="Martin Garrix">Martin Garrix</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dash_Berlin" title="Dash Berlin">Dash Berlin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Julian_Jordan" title="Julian Jordan">Julian Jordan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicky_Romero" title="Nicky Romero">Nicky Romero</a>, <a href="/wiki/W%26W" title="W&amp;W">W&amp;W</a>, <a href="/wiki/Don_Diablo" title="Don Diablo">Don Diablo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ummet_Ozcan" title="Ummet Ozcan">Ummet Ozcan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Headhunterz" title="Headhunterz">Headhunterz</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sander_van_Doorn" title="Sander van Doorn">Sander van Doorn</a> and <a href="/wiki/Afrojack" title="Afrojack">Afrojack</a>; the first four of which have been ranked as best in the world by <a href="/wiki/DJ_Mag_Top_100_DJs" class="mw-redirect" title="DJ Mag Top 100 DJs">DJ Mag Top 100 DJs</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam_Dance_Event" title="Amsterdam Dance Event">Amsterdam Dance Event</a> (ADE) is the world's leading electronic music conference and the biggest club festival for the many electronic subgenres on the planet.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The Netherlands has <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Netherlands in the Eurovision Song Contest">participated</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a> since its first edition in 1956, and has won five times.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Classical_music" title="Classical music">classical music</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Sweelinck" class="mw-redirect" title="Jan Sweelinck">Jan Sweelinck</a> ranks as the most famous Dutch composer, with <a href="/wiki/Louis_Andriessen" title="Louis Andriessen">Louis Andriessen</a> amongst the best known contemporary Dutch classical composers. <a href="/wiki/Ton_Koopman" title="Ton Koopman">Ton Koopman</a> is a Dutch conductor, organist and harpsichordist. Notable violinists are <a href="/wiki/Janine_Jansen" title="Janine Jansen">Janine Jansen</a> and <a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Rieu" title="André Rieu">André Rieu</a>. The latter, together with his <a href="/wiki/Johann_Strauss_Orchestra" title="Johann Strauss Orchestra">Johann Strauss Orchestra</a>, has taken classical and <a href="/wiki/Waltz_(music)" title="Waltz (music)">waltz music</a> on worldwide concert tours, the size and revenue of which are otherwise only seen from the world's biggest rock and pop music acts.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film_and_television">Film and television</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_the_Netherlands" title="Cinema of the Netherlands">Cinema of the Netherlands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Television_in_the_Netherlands" title="Television in the Netherlands">Television in the Netherlands</a></div>
<p>Some Dutch films – mainly by director <a href="/wiki/Paul_Verhoeven" title="Paul Verhoeven">Paul Verhoeven</a> – have received international distribution and recognition, such as <i><a href="/wiki/Turkish_Delight_(1973_film)" title="Turkish Delight (1973 film)">Turkish Delight</a></i> ("<i>Turks Fruit</i>", 1973), <i><a href="/wiki/Soldier_of_Orange" title="Soldier of Orange">Soldier of Orange</a></i> ("<i>Soldaat van Oranje</i>", 1977), <i><a href="/wiki/Spetters" title="Spetters">Spetters</a></i> (1980) and <i><a href="/wiki/The_Fourth_Man_(1983_film)" title="The Fourth Man (1983 film)">The Fourth Man</a></i> ("<i>De Vierde Man</i>", 1983). Verhoeven then went on to direct big <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_the_United_States" title="Cinema of the United States">Hollywood</a> movies like <i><a href="/wiki/RoboCop" title="RoboCop">RoboCop</a></i> (1987), <i><a href="/wiki/Total_Recall_(1990_film)" title="Total Recall (1990 film)">Total Recall</a></i> (1990) and <i><a href="/wiki/Basic_Instinct" title="Basic Instinct">Basic Instinct</a></i> (1992), and returned with Dutch film <i><a href="/wiki/Black_Book_(film)" title="Black Book (film)">Black Book</a></i> ("<i>Zwartboek</i>", 2006).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Other well-known Dutch film directors are <a href="/wiki/Jan_de_Bont" title="Jan de Bont">Jan de Bont</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Speed_(1994_film)" title="Speed (1994 film)">Speed</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Anton_Corbijn" title="Anton Corbijn">Anton Corbijn</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/A_Most_Wanted_Man_(film)" title="A Most Wanted Man (film)">A Most wanted Man</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Dick_Maas" title="Dick Maas">Dick Maas</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/De_Lift" class="mw-redirect" title="De Lift">De Lift</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Fons_Rademakers" title="Fons Rademakers">Fons Rademakers</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/The_Assault_(1986_film)" title="The Assault (1986 film)">The Assault</a></i>), and documentary makers <a href="/wiki/Bert_Haanstra" title="Bert Haanstra">Bert Haanstra</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joris_Ivens" title="Joris Ivens">Joris Ivens</a>. Film director <a href="/wiki/Theo_van_Gogh_(film_director)" title="Theo van Gogh (film director)">Theo van Gogh</a> achieved international notoriety in 2004 when he was murdered by <a href="/wiki/Mohammed_Bouyeri" title="Mohammed Bouyeri">Mohammed Bouyeri</a> in the streets of Amsterdam after directing the short film <i><a href="/wiki/Submission_(2004_film)" title="Submission (2004 film)">Submission</a></i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Internationally, successful directors of photography from the Netherlands are <a href="/wiki/Hoyte_van_Hoytema" title="Hoyte van Hoytema">Hoyte van Hoytema</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Interstellar_(film)" title="Interstellar (film)">Interstellar</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Spectre_(2015_film)" title="Spectre (2015 film)">Spectre</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Dunkirk_(2017_film)" title="Dunkirk (2017 film)">Dunkirk</a></i>) and <a href="/wiki/Theo_van_de_Sande" title="Theo van de Sande">Theo van de Sande</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Wayne%27s_World" title="Wayne&#39;s World">Wayne's World</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Blade_(1998_film)" title="Blade (1998 film)">Blade</a></i>). Van Hoytema went to the <a href="/wiki/National_Film_School_in_%C5%81%C3%B3d%C5%BA" class="mw-redirect" title="National Film School in Łódź">National Film School in Łódź</a> (<a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a>) and Van de Sande went to the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Film_Academy" title="Netherlands Film Academy">Netherlands Film Academy</a>. Internationally successful Dutch actors include <a href="/wiki/Famke_Janssen" title="Famke Janssen">Famke Janssen</a> (<a href="/wiki/X-Men_(film_series)" title="X-Men (film series)"><i>X-Men</i></a>), <a href="/wiki/Carice_van_Houten" title="Carice van Houten">Carice van Houten</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Game_of_Thrones" title="Game of Thrones">Game of Thrones</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Michiel_Huisman" title="Michiel Huisman">Michiel Huisman</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Game_of_Thrones" title="Game of Thrones">Game of Thrones</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Rutger_Hauer" title="Rutger Hauer">Rutger Hauer</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Blade_Runner" title="Blade Runner">Blade Runner</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Jeroen_Krabb%C3%A9" title="Jeroen Krabbé">Jeroen Krabbé</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/The_Living_Daylights" title="The Living Daylights">The Living Daylights</a></i>) and <a href="/wiki/Derek_de_Lint" title="Derek de Lint">Derek de Lint</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Three_Men_and_a_Baby" title="Three Men and a Baby">Three Men and a Baby</a></i>).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Netherlands has a well developed television market, with both multiple commercial and public broadcasters. Imported TV programmes, as well as interviews with responses in a foreign language, are virtually always shown with the original sound and subtitled. Only foreign shows for children are dubbed.<sup id="cite_ref-HaGanahl2013_262-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HaGanahl2013-262">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>TV exports from the Netherlands mostly take the form of specific formats and franchises, most notably through internationally active TV production conglomerate <a href="/wiki/Endemol" title="Endemol">Endemol</a>, founded by Dutch <a href="/wiki/Mass_media" title="Mass media">media</a> <a href="/wiki/Business_magnate" title="Business magnate">tycoons</a> <a href="/wiki/John_de_Mol_Jr." title="John de Mol Jr.">John de Mol</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joop_van_den_Ende" title="Joop van den Ende">Joop van den Ende</a>. Headquartered in <a href="/wiki/Amsterdam" title="Amsterdam">Amsterdam</a>, Endemol has around 90 companies in over 30 countries. Endemol and its subsidiaries create and run reality, talent, and game show franchises worldwide, including <i><a href="/wiki/Big_Brother_(franchise)" title="Big Brother (franchise)">Big Brother</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Deal_or_No_Deal" title="Deal or No Deal">Deal or No Deal</a></i>. John de Mol later started his own company <a href="/wiki/Tien_(TV_channel)" title="Tien (TV channel)">Talpa</a> which created show franchises like <a href="/wiki/The_Voice_(TV_series)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Voice (TV series)"><i>The Voice</i></a> and <i><a href="/wiki/Utopia_(U.S._reality_TV_series)" class="mw-redirect" title="Utopia (U.S. reality TV series)">Utopia</a></i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sports_in_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Sports in the Netherlands">Sports in the Netherlands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:NED-DEN_Euro_2012_(10).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg/220px-NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="199" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg/330px-NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg/440px-NED-DEN_Euro_2012_%2810%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="656" data-file-height="593" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:NED-DEN_Euro_2012_(10).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Dutch star football players <a href="/wiki/Arjen_Robben" title="Arjen Robben">Arjen Robben</a> and <a href="/wiki/Robin_van_Persie" title="Robin van Persie">Robin van Persie</a> during a game with the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_national_football_team" title="Netherlands national football team">Netherlands</a> against <a href="/wiki/Denmark_national_football_team" title="Denmark national football team">Denmark</a> at <a href="/wiki/Euro_2012" class="mw-redirect" title="Euro 2012">Euro 2012</a></div></div></div>
<p>Approximately 4.5 million of the 16.8 million people in the Netherlands are registered in one of the 35,000 sports clubs in the country. About two-thirds of the population between 15 and 75 participate in sports weekly.<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the most popular team sport in the Netherlands, followed by <a href="/wiki/Field_hockey" title="Field hockey">field hockey</a> and <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>. Tennis, gymnastics and golf are the three most widely engaged in individual sports.<sup id="cite_ref-sport_264-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sport-264">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> Organisation of sports began at the end of the 19th century and the beginning of the 20th century. Federations for sports were established, rules were unified and sports clubs came into existence. A <a href="/wiki/NOC*NSF" title="NOC*NSF">Dutch National Olympic Committee</a> was established in 1912.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_national_football_team" title="Netherlands national football team">national football team</a> was runner-up in the <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">World Cup</a> of 1974, 1978, and 2010, and won the <a href="/wiki/UEFA_European_Championship" title="UEFA European Championship">European Championship</a> of 1988. Of <a href="/wiki/Sports_Illustrated" title="Sports Illustrated">SI</a>'s 50 greatest footballers of all time, <a href="/wiki/Johan_Cruyff" title="Johan Cruyff">Johan Cruyff</a> (#5), <a href="/wiki/Marco_van_Basten" title="Marco van Basten">Marco van Basten</a> (#19), <a href="/wiki/Ruud_Gullit" title="Ruud Gullit">Ruud Gullit</a> (#25), and <a href="/wiki/Johan_Neeskens" title="Johan Neeskens">Johan Neeskens</a> (#36) are Dutch.<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_women%27s_national_football_team" title="Netherlands women&#39;s national football team">women's national team</a> was runner-up in <a href="/wiki/2019_FIFA_Women%27s_World_Cup" title="2019 FIFA Women&#39;s World Cup">2019 World Cup</a> and won the European Championship of 2017. The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_women%27s_national_field_hockey_team" title="Netherlands women&#39;s national field hockey team">Netherlands women's field hockey team</a> won 9 out of 15 <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_Hockey_World_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="Women&#39;s Hockey World Cup">World Cups</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_national_baseball_team" title="Netherlands national baseball team">Netherlands baseball team</a> have won the <a href="/wiki/European_Baseball_Championship" title="European Baseball Championship">European championship</a> 24 times out of 33 events. The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_women%27s_national_volleyball_team" title="Netherlands women&#39;s national volleyball team">volleyball national women's team</a> won the <a href="/wiki/1995_Women%27s_European_Volleyball_Championship" title="1995 Women&#39;s European Volleyball Championship">European Championship in 1995</a> and the <a href="/wiki/2007_FIVB_Volleyball_World_Grand_Prix" title="2007 FIVB Volleyball World Grand Prix">World Grand Prix in 2007</a>.
</p><p>The Netherlands has won 266 medals at the <a href="/wiki/Summer_Olympic_Games" title="Summer Olympic Games">Summer Olympic Games</a> and another 110 medals at the <a href="/wiki/Winter_Olympic_Games" title="Winter Olympic Games">Winter Olympic Games</a>. <a href="/wiki/Joop_Zoetemelk" title="Joop Zoetemelk">Joop Zoetemelk</a> won the <a href="/wiki/1979_Vuelta_a_Espana" class="mw-redirect" title="1979 Vuelta a Espana">1979 Vuelta a Espana</a>, the <a href="/wiki/1980_Tour_de_France" title="1980 Tour de France">1980 Tour de France</a>, and the 1985 UCI World Championship. <a href="/wiki/Jan_Janssen" title="Jan Janssen">Jan Janssen</a> won the <a href="/wiki/1968_Tour_de_France" title="1968 Tour de France">1968 Tour de France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tom_Dumoulin" title="Tom Dumoulin">Tom Dumoulin</a> the <a href="/wiki/2017_Giro_d%27Italia" title="2017 Giro d&#39;Italia">2017 Giro d'Italia</a>. <a href="/wiki/Max_Verstappen" title="Max Verstappen">Max Verstappen</a>, the youngest <a href="/wiki/Formula_1" class="mw-redirect" title="Formula 1">Formula 1</a> driver to make his debut and to win a race, was the <a href="/wiki/2016_Spanish_Grand_Prix" title="2016 Spanish Grand Prix">first Dutchman to win a Grand Prix</a> and a <a href="/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix" title="2021 Abu Dhabi Grand Prix">Formula One World Drivers Championship</a>. Dutch <a href="/wiki/K-1" title="K-1">K-1</a> <a href="/wiki/Kickboxing" title="Kickboxing">kickboxers</a> have won the <a href="/wiki/K-1_World_Grand_Prix" title="K-1 World Grand Prix">K-1 World Grand Prix</a> 15 times out of 19 tournaments.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Dutch_cuisine" title="Dutch cuisine">Dutch cuisine</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output <div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG/220px-Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="176" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG/330px-Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG/440px-Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG 2x" data-file-width="739" data-file-height="591" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hutspot_met_stooflap_op_bord.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Hutspot" title="Hutspot">Hutspot</a> with meat on plate</div></div></div>
<p>Originally, the country's cuisine was shaped by the practices of <a href="/wiki/Fishing" title="Fishing">fishing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Farming" class="mw-redirect" title="Farming">farming</a>, including the cultivation of the soil for growing crops and raising domesticated animals. Dutch cuisine is simple and straightforward, and contains many dairy products. Breakfast and lunch are typically bread with toppings, with cereal for breakfast as an alternative. Traditionally, dinner consists of potatoes, a portion of meat, and (seasonal) vegetables.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Dutch diet was relatively high in carbohydrates and fat, reflecting the dietary needs of the labourers whose culture moulded the country. Without many refinements, it is best described as rustic, though many holidays are still celebrated with special foods. In the course of the twentieth century this diet changed and became much more <a href="/wiki/Multiculturalism" title="Multiculturalism">cosmopolitan</a>, with most <a href="/wiki/Global_cuisine" title="Global cuisine">global cuisines</a> being represented in the major cities.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Modern culinary writers distinguish between three general regional forms of Dutch cuisine. The regions in the northeast of the Netherlands, roughly the provinces of <a href="/wiki/Groningen_(province)" title="Groningen (province)">Groningen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Friesland" title="Friesland">Friesland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Drenthe" title="Drenthe">Drenthe</a>, <a href="/wiki/Overijssel" title="Overijssel">Overijssel</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gelderland" title="Gelderland">Gelderland</a> north of the <a href="/wiki/Grote_rivieren" title="Grote rivieren">great rivers</a> are the least populated areas of the Netherlands. The late (18th century) introduction of large scale agriculture means that the cuisine is generally known for its many kinds of meats. The relative lack of farms allowed for an abundance of <a href="/wiki/Game_(meat)" class="mw-redirect" title="Game (meat)">game</a> and <a href="/wiki/Animal_husbandry" title="Animal husbandry">husbandry</a>, though dishes near the coastal regions of Friesland, Groningen and the parts of Overijssel bordering the <a href="/wiki/IJsselmeer" title="IJsselmeer">IJsselmeer</a> also include a large amount of fish. The various dried sausages, belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Metworst" title="Metworst">metworst</a>-family of Dutch sausages are found throughout this region and are highly prized for their often very strong taste. Also smoked sausages are common, of which (<i>Gelderse</i>) <i><a href="/wiki/Rookworst" title="Rookworst">rookworst</a></i> is the most renowned. The sausage contains a lot of fat and is very juicy. Larger sausages are often eaten alongside <i><a href="/wiki/Stamppot" title="Stamppot">stamppot</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Hutspot" title="Hutspot">hutspot</a></i> or <i>zuurkool</i> (<a href="/wiki/Sauerkraut" title="Sauerkraut">sauerkraut</a>); whereas smaller ones are often eaten as a <a href="/wiki/Street_food" title="Street food">street food</a>. The provinces are also home to hard textured <a href="/wiki/Rye_bread" title="Rye bread">rye bread</a>, pastries and cookies, the latter heavily spiced with <a href="/wiki/Ginger" title="Ginger">ginger</a> or <a href="/wiki/Succade" title="Succade">succade</a> or containing small bits of meat. Various kinds of <i>Kruidkoek</i> (such as <a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groninger_koek" class="extiw" title="nl:Groninger koek">Groninger koek</a>), <i><a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fryske_d%C3%BAmkes" class="extiw" title="nl:Fryske dúmkes">Fryske dúmkes</a></i> and <i><a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/spekdik" class="extiw" title="nl:spekdik">spekdikken</a></i> (small savoury pancakes cooked in a waffle iron) are considered typical. A notable characteristic of <i>Fries roggebrood</i> (Frisian rye bread) is its long baking time (up to 20 hours), resulting in a sweet taste and a deep dark colour.<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> In terms of alcoholic beverages, the region is renowned for its many <a href="/wiki/Bitters" title="Bitters">bitters</a> (such as <i><a href="/wiki/Beerenburg" title="Beerenburg">Beerenburg</a></i>) and other high-proof liquors rather than beer, which is, apart from <i><a href="/wiki/Jenever" title="Jenever">Jenever</a></i>, typical for the rest of the country. As a coastal region, Friesland is home to low-lying grasslands, and thus has a cheese production in common with the Western cuisine. <i>Friese Nagelkaas</i> (<a href="/wiki/Friesian_Clove" title="Friesian Clove">Friesian Clove</a>) is a notable example.  The <i><a href="/wiki/Oliebol" title="Oliebol">oliebol</a></i> (in its modern form) and <i><a href="/wiki/Zeeuwse_bolus" title="Zeeuwse bolus">Zeeuwse bolus</a></i> are good examples. Cookies are also produced in great number and tend to contain a lot of butter and sugar, like <i><a href="/wiki/Stroopwafel" title="Stroopwafel">stroopwafel</a></i>, as well as a filling of some kind, mostly almond, like <i><a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gevulde_koek" class="extiw" title="nl:Gevulde koek">gevulde koek</a></i>. The traditional alcoholic beverages of this region are <a href="/wiki/Beer" title="Beer">beer</a> (strong pale lager) and <i><a href="/wiki/Jenever" title="Jenever">Jenever</a></i>, a high proof <a href="/wiki/Juniper" title="Juniper">juniper</a>-flavoured spirit, that came to be known in England as <a href="/wiki/Gin" title="Gin">gin</a>. A noted exception within the traditional Dutch alcoholic landscape, <i><a href="/wiki/Advocaat" title="Advocaat">Advocaat</a></i>, a rich and creamy liqueur made from eggs, sugar and brandy, is also native to this region.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The provinces of <a href="/wiki/North_Holland" title="North Holland">North Holland</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Holland" title="South Holland">South Holland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zeeland" title="Zeeland">Zeeland</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Utrecht_(province)" title="Utrecht (province)">Utrecht</a> and the Gelderlandic area of <a href="/wiki/Betuwe" class="mw-redirect" title="Betuwe">Betuwe</a> make up the region in which western Dutch cuisine is found. Because of the abundance of water and flat grasslands that are found here, the area is known for its many dairy products, which include prominent cheeses such as <a href="/wiki/Gouda_(cheese)" class="mw-redirect" title="Gouda (cheese)">Gouda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Leyden_cheese" title="Leyden cheese">Leyden</a> (spiced cheese with cumin), and <a href="/wiki/Edam_(cheese)" class="mw-redirect" title="Edam (cheese)">Edam</a> (traditionally in small spheres) as well as <a href="/wiki/Leerdammer" title="Leerdammer">Leerdammer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beemster_Cheese" class="mw-redirect" title="Beemster Cheese">Beemster</a>, while the adjacent <a href="/wiki/Zaanstreek" class="mw-redirect" title="Zaanstreek">Zaanstreek</a> in <a href="/wiki/North_Holland" title="North Holland">North Holland</a> has since the 16th century been known for its <a href="/wiki/Mayonnaise" title="Mayonnaise">mayonnaise</a>, typical whole-grain <a href="/wiki/Mustard_(condiment)" title="Mustard (condiment)">mustards</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Chocolate" title="Chocolate">chocolate</a> industry. Zeeland and South Holland produce a lot of butter, which contains a larger amount of <a href="/wiki/Milkfat" class="mw-redirect" title="Milkfat">milkfat</a> than most other European butter varieties.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> A by-product of the butter-making process, <i>karnemelk</i> (<a href="/wiki/Buttermilk" title="Buttermilk">buttermilk</a>), is also considered typical for this region. <a href="/wiki/Seafood" title="Seafood">Seafood</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Soused_herring" title="Soused herring">soused herring</a>, <a href="/wiki/Blue_mussel" title="Blue mussel">mussels</a> (called <i>Zeeuwse Mossels</i>, since all Dutch mussels for consumption are cleaned in Zeeland's <a href="/wiki/Oosterschelde" class="mw-redirect" title="Oosterschelde">Oosterschelde</a>), <a href="/wiki/European_eel" title="European eel">eels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oyster" title="Oyster">oysters</a> and <a href="/wiki/Shrimp" title="Shrimp">shrimps</a> are widely available and typical for the region. <i><a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kibbeling" class="extiw" title="nl:Kibbeling">Kibbeling</a></i>, once a local delicacy consisting of small chunks of battered <a href="/wiki/Whitefish_(fisheries_term)" title="Whitefish (fisheries term)">white fish</a>, has become a national <a href="/wiki/Fast_food" title="Fast food">fast food</a>, just as <a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekkerbekje" class="extiw" title="nl:Lekkerbekje">lekkerbek</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Pastries in this area tend to be quite doughy, and often contain large amounts of sugar; either caramelised, powdered or crystallised.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Southern Dutch cuisine consists of the cuisines of the Dutch provinces of <a href="/wiki/North_Brabant" title="North Brabant">North Brabant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Limburg_(Netherlands)" title="Limburg (Netherlands)">Limburg</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Flemish_Region" title="Flemish Region">Flemish Region</a> in <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>. It is renowned for its many rich pastries, soups, stews and vegetable dishes and is often called Burgundian which is a Dutch idiom invoking the rich Burgundian court which ruled the Low Countries in the Middle Ages, renowned for its splendour and great feasts. It is the only Dutch culinary region that developed an <a href="/wiki/Haute_cuisine" title="Haute cuisine">haute cuisine</a>. Pastries are abundant, often with rich fillings of cream, custard or fruits. Cakes, such as the <i><a href="/wiki/Vlaai" title="Vlaai">Vlaai</a></i> from Limburg and the <i>Moorkop</i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Bossche_Bol" class="mw-redirect" title="Bossche Bol">Bossche Bol</a></i> from Brabant, are typical pastries. Savoury pastries also occur, with the <span title="Dutch-language text"><i lang="nl">worstenbroodje</i></span> (a roll with a sausage of <a href="/wiki/Ground_beef" title="Ground beef">ground beef</a>, literally translates into sausage bread) being the most popular. The traditional alcoholic beverage of the region is beer. There are many local brands, ranging from <i><a href="/wiki/Trappist_beer" title="Trappist beer">Trappist</a></i> to <i><a href="/wiki/Kriek_lambic" title="Kriek lambic">Kriek</a></i>. 5 of the 10 <i>International Trappist Association</i> recognised breweries in the world, are located in the Southern Dutch cultural area. Beer, like wine in French cuisine, is also used in cooking; often in stews.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In early 2014, <a href="/wiki/Oxfam" title="Oxfam">Oxfam</a> ranked the Netherlands as the country with the most nutritious, plentiful and healthy food, in a comparison of 125 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-central_cities-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-central_cities_1-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-central_cities_1-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">Amsterdam is the constitutional capital, while the government and the royal family are seated in The Hague.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-co-official_languages-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-co-official_languages_3-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Apart from Dutch, English is an official language in the special municipalities of <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>, Papiamentu is an official language in the special municipality of <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, and West Frisian is an official language in the province of <a href="/wiki/Friesland" title="Friesland">Friesland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-official-and-recognised-languages_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-and-recognised-languages-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-recognised_languages-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-recognised_languages_5-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Having ratified the <a href="/wiki/European_Charter_for_Regional_or_Minority_Languages" title="European Charter for Regional or Minority Languages">European Charter for Regional or Minority Languages</a> in 1996, the Dutch government recognises Dutch Low Saxon, Limburgish, Sinte Romani and Yiddish as regional or non-territorial minority languages.<sup id="cite_ref-official-and-recognised-languages_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-and-recognised-languages-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> On 1 July 2021, Dutch Sign Language received the status of recognised language.<sup id="cite_ref-NGT_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NGT-4">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-6">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Excluding people with a Turkish background, who are included separately in this table.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The euro is used in the European part of the Netherlands and replaced the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_guilder" title="Dutch guilder">Dutch guilder</a> in 2002. The United States dollar is used in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a> and replaced the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antillean_guilder" title="Netherlands Antillean guilder">Netherlands Antillean guilder</a> in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-17">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">CET and CEST are used in the European Netherlands, and AST is used in the Caribbean Netherlands.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-18"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-18">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">+599 was the country code designated for the now dissolved <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>. The Caribbean Netherlands still use +599&#160;7 (for Bonaire), +599&#160;3 (for Sint Eustatius), and +599&#160;4 (for Saba).</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-19">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">.nl is the common internet top-level domain name for the Netherlands. The <a href="/wiki/.eu" title=".eu">.eu</a> domain is also used, as it is shared with other <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> member states. .bq is designated, but not in use, for the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">Caribbean Netherlands</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-235"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-235">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Only 11 stations are served less than twice an hour during weekdays.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-244"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-244">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Up from 31% vs. 19% naming the bike their main mode of transport for daily activities in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Further_reading">Further reading</span></h2>
<dl><dt>Geography and environment</dt></dl>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1054258005">.mw-parser-output .refbegin{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-hanging-indents>ul{margin-left:0}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-hanging-indents>ul>li{margin-left:0;padding-left:3.2em;text-indent:-3.2em}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-hanging-indents ul,.mw-parser-output .refbegin-hanging-indents ul li{list-style:none}@media(max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .refbegin-hanging-indents>ul>li{padding-left:1.6em;text-indent:-1.6em}}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-columns ul{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .refbegin-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}</style><div class="refbegin" style="">
<ul><li>Burke, Gerald L. <i>The making of Dutch towns: A study in urban development from the 10th–17th centuries</i>. (1960) Hassell Street Press 2021. ISBN 978-1013598852</li>
<li>Lambert, Audrey M. <i>The Making of the Dutch Landscape: An Historical Geography of the Netherlands</i> (1985) ISBN 978-0128670507; focus on the history of land reclamation</li>
<li>Meijer, Henk. <i>Compact geography of the Netherlands</i> (1985)</li>
<li>Riley, R. C., and G. J. Ashworth. <i>Benelux: An Economic Geography of Belgium, the Netherlands, and Luxembourg</i> (1975) <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.questia.com/read/85882918/benelux-an-economic-geography-of-belgium-the-netherlands">online</a></li></ul>
</div>
<dl><dt>History</dt></dl>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1054258005"/><div class="refbegin" style="">
<ul><li>Paul Arblaster. <i>A History of the Low Countries</i>. Palgrave Essential Histories Series New York: Palgrave Macmillan, 2006. 298 pp.&#160;<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-4039-4828-1" title="Special:BookSources/978-1-4039-4828-1">978-1-4039-4828-1</a>.</li>
<li>J. C. H. Blom and E. Lamberts, eds. <i>History of the Low Countries</i> (1998)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jonathan_Israel" title="Jonathan Israel">Jonathan Israel</a>. <i>The Dutch Republic: Its Rise, Greatness, and Fall 1477–1806</i>. Oxford: Clarendon Press (1995). ISBN 978-0198207344</li>
<li>J. A. Kossmann-Putto and E. H. Kossmann. <i>The Low Countries: History of the Northern and Southern Netherlands</i> (1987)</li>
<li>Amry Vandenbosch, <i>Dutch Foreign Policy since 1815</i> (1959).</li></ul>
